## v306 Phone info: wider Android support (owner: "improve it for all Androids") - version stays 0.208.0
`LiquidNative.getDeviceInfo` (fallbacks, all optional): per core cur = scaling_cur_freq -> cpuinfo_cur_freq -> its cluster policy's scaling_cur_freq (cpufreq/policyX related_cpus / affected_cpus); max = cpuinfo_max_freq -> scaling_max_freq -> policy; online (cpuN/online, cpu0 always); cap (cpu_capacity). Per policy: stats/time_in_state deltas -> AVERAGE speed over the last half second (steadier than one instant; TIS_PREV). Public APIs: battery % / temperature / voltage / plugged (sticky ACTION_BATTERY_CHANGED, Android 13+ RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED), current (BATTERY_PROPERTY_CURRENT_NOW; µA or mA by brand, normalised), heat (PowerManager thermal status 29+, headroom 30+), this app's memory (Debug.getPss every 2 s). Page: CPU load = cluster averages when shown, else instant speeds (off core = 0); core labels Eff / Perf / Prime; "off" / "hidden" per core; Battery row; Heat row; This app = CPU % + memory + threads. Research run could not start (usage); built from known device-info app practice. CHECKED: tests/phone-info-live-v305.test.mjs (+2 tests), full `npm test` 1075 pass, 7 browser checks ALL PASS (one at a time). NOT compiled here, NOT run on a phone.

## v305 Phone info: real live RAM and CPU (owner: "wrong data, especially Oppo / OnePlus; look how real apps do it") - version stays 0.208.0
WHY: the page showed "used = ActivityManager total - avail" with no total, and called this APP's own CPU time "CPU threads". Since Android 8 apps may NOT read /proc/stat (whole-phone CPU %: Google closed it as a side channel), so a true phone CPU % is impossible for a normal app; DevCheck / CPU-Z show per-core speeds instead.
WHAT: `native-android/LiquidNative.java` getDeviceInfo adds memTotalKb / memAvailKb / swapTotalKb / swapFreeKb from /proc/meminfo (swap = zram + ColorOS / OxygenOS "RAM expansion"), coreFreq [{cur, max}] per core from /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpuN/cpufreq (scaling_cur_freq; cpuinfo_max_freq or scaling_max_freq; -1 when hidden), model; NEW sysLong(). `src/main.js` Phone info (sub.phoneinfo, #lf-dev): Phone, Processor (+ cores), CPU now ("About N% of full speed" = average core speed share, asleep core = 0, hidden speeds = no number) with a bar and speed per core, Memory ("X used of Y · %" bar, "12 GB phone" nominal size, free now, swap / RAM expansion), This app (own CPU % + threads); refreshed every 500 ms while the page is open and the app is visible; a one-line note explains it. Browser demo: a moving phone-like sample (native.js mockDev).
CHECKED: NEW `tests/phone-info-live-v305.test.mjs`; full `npm test` 1073 pass; browser checks (one at a time) ALL PASS: landscape-check, landscape-split-check, landscape-rotate-hide-check, tab-lit-check, lyrics-blink-check, lyrics-open-speed-check, lyrics-switch-check. NOT compiled here (GitHub APK build does), NOT run on a phone.

## v293c read ahead covers Canvas videos and jumps too (owner: "do all 3") - version stays 0.208.0
Same looks and same picks as before (Cover blur's Canvas still uses the same 8-moment score, one frame after each seek, as lyFreezeV); only WHEN things are ready changed.
1. Cover blur + Canvas video: lyVidStillFromSrc() finds the best moment on a hidden <video> ahead and keeps it as a still (lyStillMem, by video address); lyBlurHtml draws it frozen at once (`const st = lyStillMem.get(s.src)` now for videos too).
2. Home / Song picture + Canvas video: lyVidPreload() loads the next songs' Canvas on a hidden <video> (max 5); lyBgAdopt() moves that very element (first frame ready) into the lyrics layer (lyBgSync + lyricsLoad).
3. Jumps: lyNear() = the CURRENT song first (so lyrics opened after a jump are ready), then next, next+1, previous (Repeat All wraps); opening Up Next (queueSheet) prepares its first 6 songs after those (lyPrefetchList). lyPrepOne() does one song for the style in use. `__lf.queueSheet` (browser demo only).
CHECKED: `tests/lyrics-switch-check.py` ALL PASS, now also: Canvas + Cover blur Next = picked still in frame 0; Canvas + Home / Song picture Next = video readyState 4 in frame 0; a jump 4 songs ahead from Up Next = palette in frame 0; lyrics opened a few seconds after a jump = palette at once (makes its own mp4s with ffmpeg; skipped without ffmpeg). lyrics-open-speed-check (palette identical) and lyrics-blink-check ALL PASS. `tests/lyrics-switch-v293.test.mjs` updated (+2 tests). `npm test` = 1055 pass. Battery: up to 4 songs (+6 while Up Next is open) are read ahead, staggered. NOT run on a phone. Zip NOT sent (owner: wait).

WHAT (`src/main.js`): lyPrefetch() reads the NEXT and the PREVIOUS song (play order; Repeat All wraps) right after a song starts / the lyrics open: their colours (coverTint, Web Worker, cached) and, for Cover blur, their GIF still frame (NEW lyStillFromSrc + lyStillMem, one pick per picture, max 40 kept; lyPickStill uses it too). A song not read yet: the old colours go at once (lyBlank = black, not the default red), the song's main colour comes as soon as its pixels are read (coverTint(a, t, onEarly)), then the full palette. Limit: a song skipped within a few seconds of the previous one starting may not be read yet (a long GIF takes ~2-5 s to read) - then black, its main colour, then the palette. NOT run on a phone. ## v291 split shown when turning sideways + landscape mini player hides after 8 s (owner) - version stays 0.208.0
WHY (owner): "when we flip to landscape it happens instantly, we can't see the animation" and "the mini player in landscape should disappear after 8 seconds if there is no touch on it, otherwise our animation is never used".
WHAT (`src/main.js`, `src/lsplit.js`, `src/styles/app.css`):
- Turning sideways with a song playing: matchMedia(LAND_Q) change -> `#app.lrot` holds the WIDE bar (player invisible) for the first frames, then lsplit run(1, null, { delay: 450, first }) shows the joined wide bar ~0.45 s and the player splits out. Back to portrait: the player is shown at once. Reduce Motion / style Off: no animation.
- Idle hide (landscape only): Settings > Animation > Landscape mini player > "Hide after" Never / 5 / 8 / 15 s (default 8; `set.barSplit.hide`, exported). No touch on the player for that long -> it merges back into the bar (chosen animation; `.mini.away` display:none, `#app.mini-away`), music keeps playing, the bar is full width (bar rule now `.mini:not(.leaving):not(.away)`). Not while Now Playing / lyrics cover it or a keyboard focus ring is on it (a tapped button's plain focus does not count). A touch on the player restarts the count.
- It comes back (split): swipe UP on the glass bar (the bar's press is cancelled, so no tab change), a new song, play / pause (renderMini markup changes). Portrait never hides it.
- lsplit: run(d, done, { delay, first }); queries `.mini:not(.away)`; a job whose player is gone still calls done.
CHECKED: NEW browser check `tests/landscape-rotate-hide-check.py` ALL PASS (rotate: wide bar first frame, held ~20 frames, joined split, rest gap 16; a touch restarts the count; hide at 8 s, music on, bar wide; swipe up brings it back without switching tabs; new song / pause bring it back; portrait shows it and never hides; Never; Reduce Motion; the setting saves). NEW `tests/landscape-rotate-hide-v291.test.mjs`; 3 old selector / default strings updated. landscape-split-check, tab-lit-check, landscape-check ALL PASS. `npm test` = 1045 pass. NOT run on a phone.

## v280 lyrics blink fixed (line + word-by-word "Apple style") - version stays 0.208.0
CAUSE (reproduced in Chromium, NEW `tests/lyrics-blink-check.py`): the v223 / v224 no-step-back clock only held while `Native.state.playing` was true. A phone reports "not playing" for a split second now and then (buffering, audio focus, notification refresh); the clock then fell back to the last report (up to ~0.5 s behind), the playing line / filled words stepped back and forward = the blink every few seconds. With jittery reports alone there was no blink; with the flicker: 1-3 un-fills / line steps per 12 s on every style and colour.
FIX `src/main.js` lyNow: the hold no longer needs s.playing, and "was playing" (lyLast.pl) stays set through a short not-playing report; it is cleared only by a real seek (NEW on('onSeek') resets lyClock + lyLast), a tapped line (as before) or a new song (lyricsLoad). `src/native.js` browser player: seek() now also emits onSeek like the phone player.

> **VERSION RULE (owner's order): do NOT change the version number (it is 0.208.0, set by the owner on 2026-11-12) in `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, docs or anywhere else, and do not add a "bump" to any entry. Only the owner decides when to bump it, by telling you. Every AI that edits this project must keep it as it is.**




## Palette fix session 9 (ANIMATED pictures: multi-frame repair; olive jacket, navy wall, pale-blue shirt, turquoise bed lines, eye on an OPEN-eye frame) - version stays 0.208.0
WHAT: (1) NEW `tintPaletteMultiFrame` (v223+ block of `src/main.js`, wired LAST in `tintPaletteAuto` after `tintPaletteEyeOne`, ONLY when 2+ kept frames exist, so stills are untouched). It reads every kept 96 px frame time-weighted and runs a swap search with a colour distance that counts chroma ~2x (a pale-blue shirt next to a white pillow, a navy wall next to black hair = different colours): pixels > .04 from every palette colour are binned, a bin needs >= 0.6 % of the time-weighted pixels and >= 2 frames, its TRUE mean is added (free slot, or the plain colour that costs least gives up its slot when gain > 1.15 x loss; never rank 1 / eye / extra / hair / blush / vivid / gap / < 1.2 % detail colours). Plain colours are then polished to the true mean of their own pixels, shares are recounted by nearest colour (sum = 100 %, sorted biggest first), and EVERY colour gets `fi` (the kept frame it was taken from) + `x / y` (a REAL pixel of that colour in the middle of its biggest area). One anchor frame shows as many colours as possible; a colour not well in it keeps its own best frame (2nd scene); an eye colour takes the frame whose 192 px pixels around the eye spot best match the eye hue (eye OPEN). (2) `tests/palette-harness/prep_anim.py` now SQUEEZES the whole frame to a square like the app (`tintGrab` / `tintGrabHi`) instead of centre-cropping (the crop had cut off the bed edges and the jackets, so the harness did not show what the app sees). (3) NEW `tests/palette-harness/render_multi.py` draws the red circles on the frame each colour came from (frame number printed), plus the lyrics gradient and the swatches with hex. `run_anim.mjs` exports `fi`.
FILES: src/main.js, tests/lyrics-palette-multiframe-v254.test.mjs (new, 5 tests), tests/palette-harness/{prep_anim.py,run_anim.mjs,render_multi.py}, (debug renders removed in v283, owner: docs/ keeps only screenshots + recordings) (renders of the owner's blonde_bed GIF + two_girls WebP), PALETTE_FIX_PROGRESS.txt (root + docs), UPDATES.md, docs/HANDOFF.md.
RESULT (owner's GIF 31 frames, WebP 115 frames, two scenes): share of pixels far (> .06) from every palette colour 26 % -> 9 % (GIF) and 20 % -> 15 % (WebP, the rest is plaid / outline shades); GIF now has the pale-blue shirt #d7f2f7 (6.6 %), the blue bed stripe, white pillow + grey sheet and the turquoise line colour #a3f3f0; WebP now has olive jacket #68684e, navy wall #3b4354 (separate from the black hair #484042 / #191114), brown + gold hair, skin, cream, the red sign and the pink-violet eye #a17385 circled on an open-eye frame.

## Cover palette v240: no look-alike colours + hair / skin split (owner: "it takes the same colour so many times"; no version change)
**WHAT:** `src/main.js`: `tintPaletteAuto` is now `tintPaletteDedupe(tintPaletteSplitBig(tintPaletteAutoRaw(d, hiW), d, hiW))`. `tintPaletteAutoRaw` is the old `tintPaletteAuto`, unchanged. NEW `tintPaletteDedupe` (last step of every palette): two plain colours closer than .06 (palette distance, L weight .6, a/b weight 1.6) become one; a small detail (eye, extra, blush or < 3 % share) is dropped when closer than .06 to any other colour, or within 22 deg of the same hue and closer than .10 (a darker shade of skin / a bed stripe taken for an eye); two eyes of one hue family (30 deg, < .12) are one eye colour; a blush is never merged with a non-blush; rank 1 is never dropped. NEW `tintPaletteSplitBig` (free slot only, needs the kept 96x96 frames): the biggest plain colour (>= 25 %) is cut by 2-means (light / dark half) when both halves are >= 10 % and >= .065 apart, so gold hair and pale skin stop being one beige.
**WHY:** every earlier step reads the picture on its own, so the same skin shade, the same olive eye (both eyes) or a bed stripe could be added twice; 1000314750.gif showed hair, skin and eye repeated. Measured with the real code on the GIF (6 resize variants): 6 to 7 colours before, with 2 olive eyes and one beige for hair + skin; after: skin, gold hair, skin shadow, bed blue, bed white, blush, one eye. The 16 demo pictures are unchanged except: ai.gif 10 -> 8 (a second black and a second brown), c65 / p6 lose a duplicate near-black.
**FILES:** `src/main.js`, `tests/lyrics-palette-nodupes-v240.test.mjs` (7 tests), `UPDATES.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`.
**DO NOT REDO:** do not lower the dedupe distance below the split distance (.065 split vs .06 dedupe: if dedupe were higher it would glue the two halves back together); do not move the dedupe before the extras / eyes / iris steps (it must be LAST); do not exempt eyes from the 2-eye rule (the left and right eye are one colour).

## Cover palette, PART 7 (FINAL): stills get the eye / dark hair / earring / iris steps, free slots only (100 %; no version change)
**WHAT:** `tintPaletteAddEyes`, `tintPaletteAddExtras` and `tintPaletteAddIris` now accept ONE frame (a still picture; before they returned at `frames.length < 3`). For a still the "stable in >= 20 % of the frames" gate becomes "present in the frame(s) it has". `tintPaletteAuto` frames branch chains `AddExtras(AddEyes(AddHues(...)))` and `AddIris` (when `d.hi.eyeFrames` exists); `tintImagePx` (still branch) keeps `d.hi.eyeFrames = [tintGrabHi(im, 192)]`.
**SAFETY RULES for a still (frames.length < 3):** it only FILLS FREE SLOTS: no colour is dropped, no re-ranking, no chroma boost of the dull colour, no blush-merge. ## Cover palette, animated GIF / WebP eyes, PART 4 (purple iris found; no version change)
**WHAT:** new `tintPaletteAddEyes(pal, frames, W)` (v208 eyes, in the v223+ block). An iris is a small plum / mauve spot (OKLab hue 300 to 25 deg, chroma >= .03, lightness .25 to .85) that stays in the same place over at least 20 % of the sampled frames. The most stable such spot is added as one palette colour, with its chroma-weighted colour. It fills a free slot, or replaces the smallest colour that is not rank 1, not a blush, and not an eye added in this call. It is skipped if the palette already holds a plum / mauve colour (not a blush). `tintPaletteAuto` calls it only on the ANIMATED path (after `tintPaletteAddHues`). Stills are unchanged.
**WHICH:** `src/main.js` (new function before `tintPaletteAuto`; the animated branch of `tintPaletteAuto`); `tests/lyrics-palette-eyes-v208.test.mjs` (new, 3 tests).
**WHY:** the owner says the purple eyes are missing. The 96x96 grid averages the left girl's iris (plum/mauve pixels such as `8b5668`, `cc9cac`) into white sclera and skin, so the chroma gate and the hue bins never saw it. Measured on the walking WebP (`1000314751.webp`): 96 px hue of the iris pixels is 357 deg, while skin, hair and beige are 75 to 90 deg, so a hue gate separates them. The left iris is stable at about (30,31) in 11 of 24 sampled frames.
**RESULT (walking WebP, animated path, via the real code):** before = beige, olive, grey-blue background, blush, brown hair. After = beige, olive, grey-blue background, blush, PLUM IRIS (hue 357, L .59, at x .32 y .32). The brown hair is dropped to make room (owner: decide eyes vs brown hair; eyes get priority over the earring).
**NOT DONE (open):** (1) the RIGHT girl's green-grey / olive-gold iris is still missing: its hue (about 80 to 90 deg, low chroma) is the same as skin, so the hue gate cannot separate it; it needs its own rule. (2) the earring (a mauve-blue blob near the right girl's jaw, found in about 10 frames) is NOT picked, because only ONE eye spot is picked per clip for now (a second spot found was the earring). (3) stills get no eye yet (one frame cannot prove a spot is stable). (4) on phones the animated decode uses ImageDecoder; the 96 px hue frames are the same ones the existing hue pass uses, so no decoder change was made.
**DO NOT REDO:** the eviction must never remove an eye added in the same call (an eye has a tiny share, so it was the 'smallest' and got evicted at first). ## Cover blur: GIF / animated WebP / Canvas video were too bright, now darker with detail kept (no version change, still 0.208.0)
**WHAT:** owner: GIFs in Cover blur were too bright (white lyrics lost contrast) and the faint blush / eyes were washed out. `lyBlurHtml` (`src/main.js`) adds class `lb-anim` to `.lyr-blur` for a video or a .gif / .webp / .apng source. `src/styles/app.css` new `.lyr-blur.lb-anim` rules: base `brightness(.82)` (was 1.08), detail layer `blur(4px) contrast(1.15) brightness(.9)` opacity .8 (was blur 7px, brightness 1.25, .72), veil .30 > .40 > .50 (was .10 > .16 > .22). **STILL pictures are unchanged.** FOLLOW-UP 6 (owner: blush missing in the walking-scene WebP): frame picker now looks at up to 24 frames (was 8) and weighs blush pixels more (`pink / 20`, brightness penalty `/ 12`). Not re-rendered (owner asked for the zip only). FOLLOW-UP 5 (owner: do it for Canvas video too, and for a phone without ImageDecoder): `src/main.js` now has `lyScore` (blush + brightness score), `lyPickStill` (fetch, magic-byte check, ImageDecoder best of 8 frames, else `lySample`: picture hidden, plays ~3 s, a frame scored every 250 ms, best kept), `lyFreeze` (base + detail layer get the SAME still, picked once via `parent._st`) and `lyFreezeV` (Canvas video: no autoplay / loop, 8 moments seeked and scored, both layers rest on the best one via `parent._vt`). Tested in Chromium: ImageDecoder path, forced no-ImageDecoder path (deleted `window.ImageDecoder`), WebM videos. Seen only in the test page: with several GIFs + videos decoding at once the video pick can stall; in the app only one background is active. NOT yet tried on a real phone WebView. FOLLOW-UP 4 (owner: does it work for ALL gifs): a picture you set can be a `blob:` URL with no .gif ending, so `lyBlurHtml` now also hooks `lyFreeze` for every `blob:` source; `lyFreeze` reads the first bytes (GIF header / WebP `ANIM`), adds `.lb-anim` to the parent only for animated files and leaves JPEG / PNG / still WebP alone. Tested in Chromium with blob URLs: GIF + WebP frozen, JPEG untouched. FOLLOW-UP 3 (owner: the walking-scene WebP had no blush in its first frame): `window.lyFreeze` (`src/main.js`) now decodes up to 8 evenly spaced frames with `ImageDecoder`, scores each on a 64 px copy (pink blush pixels minus a brightness penalty) and keeps the best as the still; no `ImageDecoder` or any error falls back to the first frame. Render: `gif_best_frame.png`. Not yet timed on a phone (long GIFs: 8 decodes + one fetch of the file). FOLLOW-UP 2 (owner: GIFs still too bright, eye colour and blush not showing): `.lb-anim` values now base `brightness(.5) saturate(1.9)`, detail layer `blur(3px) saturate(3) contrast(1.15) brightness(.58)` opacity .92, veil .30 > .40 > .55. Render: `gif_fix_before_after.png` (left old, right new); the blush and the eyes of both test GIFs show. Stills unchanged. FOLLOW-UP (owner: GIFs must NOT play in Cover blur): `window.lyFreeze` (`src/main.js`) draws the first frame onto a canvas (max 320 px) and swaps the `<img>` for that still (`onload="lyFreeze(this)"` on animated sources only); Canvas `<video>` has no autoplay / loop and pauses on its first frame. Test `tests/lyrics-two-styles.test.mjs` markup checks updated. A cross-origin https picture could taint the canvas, then it simply keeps playing (not seen on file / blob / same-origin). **DO NOT REDO:** keep the 48px base rule and `.lb-d`. Rendered in desktop Chromium only (`gif_before_after.png`), not on a phone.

It picks up to 6 real colours from the dominant colours of the 16x16 cells (black hair included) plus up to 3 accent colours (blush, earring, iris), lays each as its own smooth region at its place (max-normalised density map, Gaussian smoothing, power 2.4 so the nearest colour wins and complementary colours do not mix to grey). Accents never change how the main colours are split. Tests `tests/tint-detail-v225.test.mjs`: three checks relaxed / changed to the new design (far cells almost untouched; the v227 "dark patch turns orange" check is now "a big dark patch stays dark", owner's order).

## Cover colour mix: session 5, "GIFs too blurry, remove the size limit, use every frame, gradient not blur" (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **OWNER'S REPORT:** lyrics background from a GIF came out far too blurry; remove the file size limit; however many frames a GIF has, combine them all into the gradient; "I hate the blur, we just need gradients" (the gradient still comes FROM the song's / album's custom picture, GIF or Canvas). Stills were fine. The owner will send pictures and GIFs to check the look.
- **WHAT (files):** `src/main.js`, `src/tintdetail.js`, tests (below). (1) `tintDecodeFrames(src, stale)`: NO limits any more. Removed: the 90 MB file cap, the 40 / 16 frame cap (`pickCandidates`), the 3.5 s time budget, and the 12 "most different" key frames (`pickKeyFrames`). Every frame is decoded and fed one at a time into the NEW `detailAccumulator()` (`src/tintdetail.js`; `detailFromFrames` is now a wrapper around it, same result), so memory does not grow with the frame count (only 4 KB of 32x32 pixels per frame is kept). Each frame counts as long as it is on screen (`image.duration`; `d.fw` is used by `tintMeshFromPixels`). The loop yields to the UI every 8 frames and stops when the song / picture changed (`S.stale`, set in `coverTint`). (2) `tintVideoPx` (Canvas video): a frame every 0.25 s over the whole video (min 7, no cap; was 7 / 10 / 12), time allowed grows with the frame count. (3) `tintMeshGrad(m)` rewritten: no radial glows (they were the blur, and on GIFs they came from colours not in the picture). Now: the mesh is cut into at most 4 real colours (weighted k-means in OKLab), ordered along the best top-to-bottom direction (165-195 deg), CSS linear-gradient with 3 OKLab in-between colours per pair, plus a bottom veil (`rgba(0,0,0,.38)`) for the white lyrics. One-colour picture = plain 2-stop gradient. `--lyr-mix` is still one CSS value, so `app.css` is untouched.
- **CHECKED:** `tests/lyrics-gradient-v230.test.mjs` (new), `tests/tint-detail-v225.test.mjs` (accumulator == detailFromFrames, 3000-frame feed, no caps), `tests/tint-follows-canvas-v208.test.mjs` + `tint-detail-v225` wiring tests changed to the new code. Looked at the rendered gradients on the demo pictures and `ai.gif` (57 frames) in Chromium.
- **FOLLOW-UP (same session, owner: Live at SoFi too far from the cover; blush too strong in a GIF where it is rare, invisible where it is in every frame):** (a) `tintMeshSnap`: accent strength was `min(.8, .32 + cov*4)`, a floor of .32 even for a tiny coverage; now `min(.8, cov*6)`, so an accent counts as much as it is on screen (time-weighted over all frames). (b) `tintMeshGrad`: up to 5 colours, small clusters kept (>0.5 % of the picture), merge distance .04, each colour sits where it really is along the axis (min gap 12 %), and a colour covering little of the picture melts into its neighbours (`pres = sqrt(share / .05)`). All general, no picture-specific code. Test added in `tests/lyrics-gradient-v230.test.mjs`.
- **NOT TESTED ON A PHONE:** `ImageDecoder` does not exist in Node, so the real GIF decode loop (`tintDecodeFrames`) was only checked by reading + the lab feeding the same accumulator. A GIF of several 100 MB is held fully in memory while it is read (`arrayBuffer`); if a phone ever runs out of memory there, the catch falls back to the first frame.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not put radial glows or a blurred picture back (that is the blur the owner hates); do not bring back a file / frame / time cap; do not keep frames in an array (use the accumulator). **STILL OPEN:** the look is judged by eye; the owner sends pictures / GIFs. Tuning knobs, all in `tintMeshGrad`: colour count (4), direction range (165-195), veil strength (.38), merge distance (.05).

## Cover colour mix: session 4, "too blurry, should be a beautiful gradient" (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **OWNER'S REPORT:** the mix looked like a blurred copy of the picture with dark blobs (a face, hair) instead of a gradient. **WHAT (`tintMeshSnap`, src/main.js):** (1) the snapshot follows the picture's lightness less (.4 -> .16) and is blurred more (lightness 3 passes, colour 2); (2) NEW colour bleeding before the accents: grey / dark cells take their colour from colourful neighbours (4 passes of a chroma-weighted 5x5 blur) and keep only half of their own darkness; (3) accents unchanged (still last). Picture: `docs/screenshots/colour-mix-gradient-v227.png` (row 1 picture, 2 v225, 3 v226 look with the new blur, 4 now). GIF frames: `docs/screenshots/gif-backgrounds-v227.png` (key frames, each alone, and the weighted result the app uses).
- **HOW IT WORKS FOR GIF / WEBP / CANVAS (unchanged, say it plainly):** ONE static background per cover = the weighted mix of up to 12 key frames, not a background that animates frame by frame. A webp with two different scenes (the pair file has a second scene of legs / skirts) blends both. If the owner wants a background that follows the animation, that is a new ticket.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not remove the bleeding or go back to lightness .4 (dark blobs return). **STILL OPEN:** look is judged by eye on ~12 pictures only; "perfect for any size / length" is NOT proven (only the existing 96 px grab, 40 candidate frames, 3.5 s budget, 90 MB cap); nothing run on a phone.

## Cover colour mix: session 3 (finer 16x16 grid, two accents per cell, soft accent, accent budget, lighter decode) (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **WHAT:** (1) `CELLS` is now 16 (was 12): the snapshot and the accent grid are 6 px cells of the 96 px frame. (2) `detailFromFrames` keeps accent strength PER HUE FAMILY of a cell and returns `acc` (strongest) and `acc2` (second, a hue family 60+ deg away). (3) `tintMeshSnap` (src/main.js) lays both accents on SOFTLY: the cell gets weight t, its 4 neighbours .38 t, diagonals .14 t (before: one cell overwritten after the blur, a visible block); the hue-gap test looks at the cell colour before any accent; t = min(.8, .32 + cov * 4). (4) Thresholds are in one exported object `ACC` (`src/tintdetail.js`). New: a clearly coloured SMALL spot (dC > .09, chroma > .08) gets looser edge tests (std .12, 3 of 9 neighbours) but must reach sideways AND up/down (a 1 px line never counts). (5) Accent BUDGET `ACC.cap = .11`: at most 11% of the cells (28 of 256) keep an accent, strongest first; before, busy anime frames lit 30 to 60 cells. (6) Decode / memory: `tintGrabHi` re-uses 3 canvases (`tintHiCv`) instead of making 1 to 4 new ones per frame, `tintHiFree()` releases them when `tintPixels` finishes, every `VideoFrame` is closed in a `finally`, the `ImageDecoder` is always closed, `completeFramesOnly: true`, files over 90 MB are not decoded (first frame is used).
- **FILES:** `src/tintdetail.js`, `src/main.js` (`tintMeshSnap`, `tintGrabHi`, `tintDecodeFrames`, `tintPixels`), `tests/tint-detail-v225.test.mjs` (12 -> 17 tests, the v225 `d.n` 12 -> 16), `tests/lab-tint/` (new: `prep.py`, `lab.mjs`, `sweep.mjs`, `sheet.py`, see below), `docs/screenshots/colour-mix-accent-v226.png` (rows: picture / v225 / v226 / accent cells of v225 / accent cells of v226).
- **MEASURED (Node, real functions, 9 distinct pictures from `tests/fixtures/demo-pics` + crops of the owner's two pictures cut from the v225 screenshot; the demo folder has duplicates and grey covers, so it is fewer than it looks):** distance of the background to the picture (chroma plane) is the same or lower than v225 (bed girl .026 -> .023, pair .029 -> .030, Starboy-like .056 -> .057); accent cells per picture went from 30 to 75 (up to 98 with the looser small-spot rule) to at most 28 + the second layer; the bed girl's cheeks and the pair image's navy wall at the right edge are still accented. Run time of `detailFromFrames` at 96 px with 12 frames: 5 to 60 ms in Node, 180 ms for the 57-frame GIF (12 key frames) (a phone is maybe 3 to 5x slower).
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not go back to 12x12 or to one accent that overwrites a block; do not remove the cap or the both-directions test (without them hair highlights and outlines come back); keep accents AFTER the blur; keep `tintMeshFromPixels(d)` with one argument identical to v223.
- **STILL OPEN (honest list):** (a) the pair image's blue EARRING is a 1 to 2 px wide strip at 96 px, so it is not told apart from a thin line: it needs a bigger detail frame (192 px = 4x the cost) or a special case; the purple EYE is only found with `ACC.sup = 6`, which doubles false accents, so it is left off. (b) Thresholds are tuned on those few pictures only; there is no real ground truth for "false accent" (judged by looking at `colour-mix-accent-v226.png` and by counts), so more pictures (photos, album covers, long GIFs) are still welcome: `cd tests/lab-tint; python3 prep.py; node lab.mjs ../.. new; node sweep.mjs`. (c) NOT RUN ON A PHONE: decode speed of a long GIF with `ImageDecoder`, real memory use, `drawImage` quality. The changes above are written to lower the risk (canvas re-use, closes, size cap, existing 3.5 s budget) but are only checked by tests that read the source text. Seeking far into a long GIF with `ImageDecoder` may cost more than the budget on some WebViews: measure it first if it feels slow.

## Cover colour mix: detail pass (faint blush, small accents) + smarter GIF / video frame reading (no version change, still 0.208.0) [SESSION 1 of a multi-session job]
- **WHY (owner's report):** a faint pink blush on a character's cheeks, the blue stripes of a bed and similar small details were missing from the lyrics background; colours looked wrong. MEASURED on the owner's two files (`1000314750.gif` bed girl, `1000314751.webp` two girls): the blush is hue 9 deg on skin of hue 38 deg and only ~0.35% of the picture, i.e. 3-4 pixels in the 32x32 grab, then averaged with skin / hair inside a 5x5-pixel grid cell; and a cell that holds blue stripe + orange hair was AVERAGED into tan.
- **WHAT:** (1) NEW `src/tintdetail.js` (pure, no DOM): `detailFromFrames(frames, weights)` reads 96x96 frames and gives per 6x6 cell (a) the DOMINANT colour = the biggest hue family of the cell (12 hue bins), not an average, and (b) an ACCENT = small smooth areas whose hue differs from their surroundings (pixel vs blurred neighbourhood; must be a blob, not an edge / thin line, not a big area). `pickCandidates(n, cap)`, `frameSig`, `pickKeyFrames(sigs, keep, minGap, wts)` choose GIF frames. (2) `src/main.js`: `tintGrabHi` (96x96, stepwise halving), `tintDecodeFrames` rewritten (all frames of a GIF up to 40; longer ones looked at in up to 40 places over the whole run, 16 if the file is over 25 MB; 3.5 s time budget; frame durations from `image.duration`; then at most 12 most-different key frames, each weighted by the stretch it stands for), `tintVideoPx` (7 / 10 / 12 frames by length, 9 s timeout), `tintImagePx` (stills get a 96 px frame too). The detail rides on the pixel array as `d.hi = { frames, w }` (`tintJoin` keeps it). `tintMeshFromPixels(d, hd)` has a new OPTIONAL second argument: with it, dominant colours replace the cell averages early (faint hues such as navy get a minimum chroma so the neutral-lift step does not erase them) and accents are applied LAST (after the centre pull / lifts, before the final blur). `coverTint` passes `detailFromFrames(...)` in.
- **FILES:** `src/tintdetail.js` (new), `src/main.js`, `tests/tint-detail-v225.test.mjs` (new), docs.
- **DO NOT REDO:** keep `tintMeshFromPixels(d)` with ONE argument identical to v223 (old tests / fixtures rely on it). Do not change `tintFromPixels` (Auto picker); it only receives better-chosen GIF frames now. Do not go back to equally spaced frames by index or to 8 frames. Do not make accents area-based (the blush is 0.17% of the picture); they are found by local hue difference + size test + blob test. Keep the 32x32 grab (`tintGrab`) as it was.
- **CHECKED:** `npm test` (see the result in the final answer). The real functions were run in Node on every frame of the owner's two files (frames cut with PIL; lab scripts are NOT in the zip). Before / after picture: `docs/screenshots/colour-mix-detail-v225.png`. NOT run on a phone: `ImageDecoder`, `drawImage` quality and decode speed on a real WebView are untested.
- **SESSION 2 (owner: "it does not look like the picture behind it any more"): FIXED.** Measured with a script (mean OKLab distance between the background and a blurred, darkened copy of the real picture): my session-1 "dominant hue family replaces the cell average" made the background FURTHER from the picture than v223 on 3 of 5 covers (Echoes .099 -> .121, Gasoline .052 -> .084). That override (and the 6x6 accent step) was REMOVED. Now `tintMeshFromPixels(d, hd)` returns `tintMeshSnap(tintMeshFromPixels(d), hd)`: the unchanged v223 6x6 mesh (contrast, no dark blob, white covers keep a tint) blended with a SNAPSHOT: the real weighted mean OKLab colour of every cell of a 12x12 grid made from the 96x96 frames (`detailFromFrames` default N is now 12 and returns `mean` next to `dom` / `acc`), colour weight .65 (not for bright colourless cells), lightness weight .4, the same vibrance as v223, a light blur, then accents (cov > .01, hue gap > 14 deg) laid on last. Distance to the picture now .072 / .074 / .040 / .051 / .057 (Starboy / Echoes / Gasoline / Blinding Lights / Too Late) against v223 .103 / .099 / .052 / .082 / .078. Frame picking (GIF / video) from session 1 is unchanged.
- **DO NOT REDO (session 2):** do not make cells take a dominant hue instead of their mean (it drifts from the picture); keep the snapshot blend; keep accents AFTER the blur. `hd.n` may be any N >= 6 (the mesh is built at hd.n, `tintMeshRender` is N-generic).
- **STILL OPEN:** the anime pictures look pale / greyish-blue as a background (pastel colours + the white-text darkening); consider a little more chroma for pastel pictures. The pair image still shows the orange girl's blush only faintly and the navy wall weakly. Accent threshold tuned on 7 pictures only (session 3: see the entry above). Not run on a phone (ImageDecoder speed, memory, `drawImage` quality, how the 12x12 mesh looks behind real lyrics).
- (old session-1 list, partly done) **NEXT SESSION (where session 1 stopped):** (a) [still open] the pair image (`.webp`): the orange girl's cheek blush is too faint to show, the navy wall behind them and the purple eyes are still missing (the wall is mostly covered by hair, the rest is dark low-chroma); try a finer grid for the detail layer or a second accent per cell. (b) The accent threshold `cov > .008` and the hue gap 14 deg are tuned on 2 pictures only; test on more (anime frames, photos, album covers) and watch for false accents (hair highlights). (c) The lyrics background looks a little less smooth than v223 around the accent (a 6x6 grid): consider a second accent pass at 12x12. ## Landscape glass pill, Settings gear button, customizable tab bar (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **WHAT:** (1) The tab-bar glass pill was capped by the bar height: the landscape bar is 58 px, so Pill size / Magnification / Wobble up-down barely did anything sideways (measured in Chromium: magnification 1.08x vs 1.22x in portrait at defaults, 1.41x vs 1.86x with the sliders maxed). `paint()` in `src/glidebar.js` now works out the pill's overshoot against `rref = max(barHeight, REF_BAR_H = 70)` and uses a proportional margin in the magnify fit when the bar is shorter than 70 px; portrait (78 px at 100 %) is numerically unchanged. After the fix landscape is 1.22x at defaults and 1.64x maxed. Button squash (`jelly.js`), toggles and the CSS wobbles were already identical in both orientations (measured). The landscape bar also ignored Glass bar size: `--lbar` is now `clamp(46px, 58px * --tabk, 90px)` and the landscape icon / label sizes follow `--tabk`. (2) The header's three dots (`header()` in `src/main.js`, `data-act="settings"`) is now a pill with a gear and the word "Settings" (`I.gear` in `src/icons.js`, class `.setbtn`). Do not put the pill height back to `rh`-only limits. Keep the stored key `tabs`. Lyrics blink re-check (same entry): a stress run of the real `lyNow()` with jittery reports found rare 1.0 s backward steps, so the `lyLast` hold is now 1.2 s (was .8) and tapping a lyric line resets `lyLast`; do not lower it again. Second pass (same entry): the landscape mini player (`--lmini`, 46 to 84 px) follows Glass bar size and stays centred on the bar's line; landscape tab labels are capped (.75 rem, .625 rem under 780 px wide) so five labels never collide; checked at 844x390, 700x320, 915x412 and bar sizes 50 to 220 %. ## Cover colour mix: new lyrics background (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **WHAT:** Settings > Lyrics > Background style has a third choice, "Cover colour mix". It is `set.lyricStyle === 'cover'` PLUS the new flag `set.lyricMix === true` (`html[data-lymix="1"]`); `data-lyrstyle` still only has `classic` / `cover`. `tintMeshFromPixels(d)` (src/main.js, block `v223+: COVER COLOUR MIX` after `coverTint`) reads the SAME 32x32 pixels as the Auto picker (GIF / Canvas frames folded onto the same grid cells by pixel index mod 1024) as a 6x6 grid of OKLab colours: colourful pixels weigh more (chroma^1.5, near-black / near-white hardly count), a region keeps its own average lightness (black cover + small copper detail stays dark), neutral dark patches (hair, face) are lifted toward neighbours (no dark blob; a dark but colourful patch like Starboy's blue figure stays), white / grey regions lean to the picture's overall colour (Echoes of Silence is dusty red, not grey), faint steady colours get a little vibrance, the centre colour is blended in strongest. `tintMeshRender` smooths the grid (Mitchell) into a 30x64 picture, keeps chroma between different colours, and darkens every colour to relative luminance <= .21 by lowering lightness while keeping chroma. `tintMeshUrl` turns it into a PNG data URL once per cover (kept on the cached colour, `c.mesh`, `c.mixUrl`); `lyApplyTint` sets `--lyr-mix` on `#lyr`. Mix mode ignores the picked Lyrics colour (the menu item shows as off); the plain cover style is untouched.
- **FILES:** `src/main.js`, `src/styles/app.css` (right after the cover-style rules, before the v222 landscape section), `src/settingsio.js` (label), `tests/cover-colour-mix-v223.test.mjs` (new), `tests/fixtures/{takemybreath,gasoline,afterhours,echoes,starboy}-32.b64` (new 32x32 crops), docs.

## Refresh art overlay shows the user's own pictures and GIFs (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **WHAT:** the spiral now flies up to 10 of the user's own pictures and GIFs (song, album, artist, genre and playlist pictures) into the glowing core. Without any custom picture it falls back to plain tiles.
- **HOW:** `sampleCustomPics()` copies each chosen picture with `fetch` + `URL.createObjectURL` BEFORE `refreshAllPics()` runs, because that function revokes the originals. The copies are kept in `rfCopies` and revoked when the overlay closes (`revokeRfCopies()`).
- **FILES:** `src/main.js` (`refreshShow` with pictures, `sampleCustomPics`, `revokeRfCopies`, `refreshArtRun`), `src/styles/app.css` (`.rfstage img.rfpic`).
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not take the picture URLs after `refreshAllPics()`; they are already revoked by then.
- **CHECKED:** `npm test` 792 pass, 0 fail; `vite build` ok; frames rendered in Chromium with stand-in pictures. Not run with real custom pictures on a phone.

## Refresh art has its own animation, not the library scan (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **CAUSE:** Refresh art called `loadLib(true, true)`, which runs the full library scan with the "Finding your music" screen (`scanView`).
- **FIX:** Refresh art calls `refreshArtRun()` (`src/main.js`). The folder picture re-check runs as a quiet scan (`loadLib(rescan, forceArt, quiet)`; `quiet` skips the scan screen and the "N songs" toast). The library scan screen is unchanged.
- **NEW ANIMATION:** `refreshShow()` shows ten picture tiles spiralling into a glowing core (`.rfstage`, `rfsuck`, `rfpulse`), then a check mark (`.rfcheck`, `rfpop`) for "Refresh finished" (closes after 1.8 s). Titles: "Reloading pictures and GIFs", "Checking folder pictures", "Refresh finished" / "Refresh stopped".
- **KEPT:** the pictures on screen still get the sucking-in effect (`pulsePics`, `html.pics-refreshing`).
- **FILES:** `src/main.js` (`loadLib`, `refreshShow`, `refreshArtRun`, the one-line `case 'refresh-art'`), `src/styles/app.css` (`.rfstage`, `.rfcore`, `.rfcheck`), `tests/bug15-refresh-art.test.mjs` (expects `refreshArtRun`).
- **DO NOT REDO:** keep the `case 'refresh-art'` on one line (the test reads it). Do not change the library scan screen for Refresh art.
- **CHECKED:** `npm test` 792 pass, 0 fail; `vite build` ok; the animation frames rendered in Chromium. Not run on a phone.

## Import: progress, summary, missing-song titles, save-to-file keeps the import (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **"undefined" in Not in your library:** the planner gave a missing song no title. Now `(no title)` (`src/plforeign.js`, `planForeign`).
- **Save missing songs lost the import:** the save picker closes, `visibilitychange` runs `startPopup()`, and the popup card replaced the import preview. Now the popup handler is skipped while `plPending` or `plBusy` is set, and `saveMissing` reopens the preview in `finally`.
- **Progress:** `plBusySheet()` shows a bar and spinner while matching and while importing (`plBusy`).
- **Summary:** `plSummarySheet()` after the import: new playlists, merged, songs matched, not in your library, not imported, favorites, saved yes/no.
- **FILES:** `src/plforeign.js`, `src/main.js` (`plBusy`, `plBusySheet`, `plSummarySheet`, `importPl`, `plImportOk`, `saveMissing`, the visibilitychange popup guard), `src/styles/app.css` (`.plbar`, `.plspin`, `.plstats`).
- **CHECKED:** `npm test` 792 pass, 0 fail; `vite build` ok; progress and summary sheets rendered in Chromium. Not run on a phone.

## Shuffle: Repeat All gives a new order each pass (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **CHECKED FIRST:** `shuffle.js` spreads artists and albums (simulation: same album back-to-back 2.2 times per 39 gaps, random would be about 9; two artists: about 14 of 59 gaps, random about 30). The Android `replaceTail` reorders the real playlist and ExoPlayer's own shuffle stays off, so the player and the Up Next list match.
- **GAP FOUND:** with Shuffle on and Repeat All, the list looped back to the start in the same shuffled order every time.
- **FIX:** `src/native.js` `reshuffleAfterLoop()`: when the list wraps from the last song to the first (`onTrackChanged`, Shuffle on, Repeat All, more than 2 songs), the rest of the list gets a new shuffle and `replaceTail` sends it to the player.
- **FILES:** `src/native.js`, `tests/shuffle-loop-v212.test.mjs`.
- **OPEN:** the owner's exact symptom is not known. Ask for it if shuffle still feels wrong (e.g. which songs repeat, or whether it is about Shuffle All, a playlist, or the Up Next order).
- **CHECKED:** `npm test` 787 pass, 0 fail; `vite build` ok. Not run on a phone.

## Song menu: Lyrics colour shows dimmed at once; Settings regrouped (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **CAUSE OF THE FLASH:** the disabled "Lyrics colour" row used an inline `opacity:.5`. The sheet rows play an entrance animation (`lf-item`, fades from opacity 0 to 1). An animation beats an inline style while it runs, so the row showed at full brightness for about half a second, then dropped to .5 when the animation ended.
- **FIX:** the disabled row is `class="row off"` and `#sheet .sheetbox .row.off{animation:none;opacity:.5}` (in `src/styles/app.css`). It is dimmed from the first frame.
- **SETTINGS REGROUPED:** Display = Motion, Bigger album covers, Increase Contrast, Background picture or GIF. Now Playing = Now Playing cover size, Immersive album cover, Playing bar time. Shortcuts = unchanged. Lyrics (new) = Background style, Text size, Lyrics animation. Home = Home screen only. Animation = Lens refraction, Liquid glass & wobble. Playback = unchanged. Library = Album details, Smart artist grouping, then the old Library rows.
- **FILES:** `src/main.js` (`lycItem`, the Settings render), `src/styles/app.css` (`.row.off`).
- **DO NOT REDO:** Background style belongs under Lyrics, not Display.
- **CHECKED:** `npm test` 786 pass, 0 fail; `vite build` ok; Settings section order and rows read from the built page in Chromium. Not on a phone.

## Step 2 of the accessibility cards now lands on the HOME screen (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **WHAT:** `exitApp()` in `native-android/LiquidNative.java` now starts a HOME intent (ACTION_MAIN + CATEGORY_HOME) first, then calls `finishAndRemoveTask()` (fallback `moveTaskToBack`) as before.
- **WHY:** closing the task alone makes Android reveal the previous task in the stack (e.g. YouTube) instead of home. With no other app open, the stack had only home, so it looked fine.
- **FILES:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java`, `tests/a11y-cards-v214.test.mjs` (1 new test), `UPDATES.md`, this file.
- **DO NOT REDO:** no change to the checks, the popup timing or the card logic in `src/assist.js`; do not replace the home intent with only `moveTaskToBack` (it also reveals the previous app).

## Accessibility of the permission cards: Back closes them, focus and TalkBack fixed, screenshots described (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **WHAT:** (1) Android Back and Escape close the Accessibility card (= \"Not now\", this open only, same as the button) and the background card (= \"Later\"). ## Red "Scroll down ↓" hint on EVERY scrolling card, sheet, list and page (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **v216 (OWNER, REVERSES THIS SECTION):** the hint is now ONLY on the popup cards: `#sheet .popcard` (notification / update picture popups) and `#lf-assist > div` (the accessibility instruction cards). `src/scrollhint.js` `SEL` is `'#sheet .popcard, #lf-assist > div'`. Pages, lists, sheets, grids and the picker sheets have NO hint. Test: `tests/scroll-hint-popups-v216.test.mjs`. **DO NOT REDO:** do not put the hint back on pages, lists or sheets; the rest of this section is history.
- **WHAT (owner's order):** people did not know there is more below. New `src/scrollhint.js` (`startScrollHints()` called in `main.js` next to `startAssist()`; `addScrollHint(el)` used by `assist.js`). It watches `#sheet .sheetbox` (all sheets: Listening-stats snapshot / clear / range, pickers, info), `.picklist`, `.infobox`, `.qlist`, album-picker `.grid`, `.screen` (pages and settings sub pages) and the accessibility cards (`#lf-assist > div`).
- **HOW:** one `position:fixed` red bold "Scroll down ↓" per scroller (dark fade, `pointer-events:none`, `aria-hidden`), at the bottom of the scroller's VISIBLE part; on pages it sits above the mini player / tab bar. Shown only while more than 24 px is left below; hidden at the bottom, when the content fits, when the scroller is hidden, or when a page is covered by a sheet / Now Playing / lyrics / card. Removed with its scroller. Sweep every 400 ms + on scroll/resize/image load.
- **NOT TOUCHED:** card texts, buttons, flow, version. Not verified on a real phone (checked in headless Chromium at 393x800 with the real app.css).

## Accessibility cards first, then the background card; "Back to the first card"; shorter bottom hint (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **OWNER'S ORDER:** when both are due, the Accessibility cards come FIRST and the "Allow background activity" card only 10 s after they are finished or dismissed (Not now). Never two cards at once (before, both could pass their 10 s gap in the same second, and after the first Accessibility tap the background card came on top of the steps).
- **HOW:** `src/assist.js` exports `assistHoldsBg()` (true while the sequence still has a card to show this open: helper OFF, stage < 3, and something happened in the last 45 s = `HOLD_IDLE_MS`). `src/bgallow.js` has `setBgAllowAfter(fn)`: while `fn()` is true its `readyAt` keeps moving 10 s ahead, and `maybeShow()` checks `accessibilityFirst()` again after Android answers, right before it builds. `src/main.js`: `setBgAllowAfter(assistHoldsBg)` next to `startAssist()`. `assist.js` also checks `lf-bgallow` right before it builds (so the two checks cannot both pass).
- **FAIL-SAFE (so v211's "zero popups" bug cannot come back):** the hold lasts only while the sequence is alive (card shown / closed / person came back / its gate just opened in the last 45 s). If the accessibility cards never get their turn the background card still comes after about 45 s (+10 s gap, +8 s when its picture cannot be read). `assist.js` `quickCheck()` looks at the helper switch silently at start and on every return to the app, so a helper that is already ON never holds the background card back.
- **BACKGROUND DETECTION UNCHANGED:** `LiquidNative.isBackgroundAllowed` still answers `allowed = ignoring` (battery-optimization whitelist, confirmed on OnePlus / OPPO by adb); JS side unchanged (4 s limit, re-check every 5 s while allowed, every open while OFF). NOT verified on a real phone in this session.
- **STEP 2 HELP (owner's order):** the step 2 card has a help box: "Do not see Allow restricted settings?" + the owner's screenshot (`ASSIST_PICS.s1` in `src/assistpics.js`: App info, three dots only show "Uninstall for all users", red box) + the fix text (step 1 again: Accessibility > Downloaded apps > tap the greyed-out Liquid Dark battery helper > Restricted setting > OK, then step 2 again). Its button: "Menu only shows \"Uninstall for all users\"? Back to step 1". **DO NOT REDO:** keep the screenshot and the text; the button must stay `data-as="back1"`.
- **BACKGROUND CARD PICTURE:** unchanged and verified in the code: `bgallow.js` builds the card with the picture from `background-allow-gif-pic/` on GitHub (`latest.json` polled every second by `bgallowpic.js`); the zip's folder is empty on purpose, the owner's picture lives in the repo and the workflow never deletes it when a zip is unpacked.
- **BACK BUTTONS (final labels):** step 2 card: "Back to step 1". Step 3 card: "Back to step 2 (switch greyed out?)" (always shown now, also when Android says restricted settings are allowed) and "Back to step 1".
- **BACK BUTTON:** the Step 2 and Step 3 cards have "Back to the first card (step 1)" (`data-as=\"back1\"`): step 1 comes at once, saved progress is cleared, and Android's restricted-settings answer is ignored until the next open (`rsIgnore`, also set by the step 3 "greyed out" button).
- **BOTTOM HINT:** `LiquidNative.openBackgroundSettings`, App-info route: ONE `Toast.LENGTH_SHORT` (about 2 s) with the shorter text "Tap Battery usage, then turn on Allow background activity." (was four LONG toasts, about 12 s).
- **TESTS:** `tests/bg-reopen-after-trip-v212.test.mjs` (accessibility-first order, full 3-step flow, fail-safe, back button, no overlap), `tests/permchain-sim.mjs` (wires `setBgAllowAfter`, new scenarios `back1Step2`, `back1Step3`), `tests/background-power-page.test.mjs` (one short toast), `tests/battery-assist.test.mjs`, `tests/permission-cards-every-open-v208.test.mjs`, Toast stubs in `tests/java-probe*.py`. Full suite: 761 pass.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not remove the 45 s fail-safe (that is what v213 fixed); do not make the background card wait for anything except `assistHoldsBg()` and the notification gate; keep the toast single and short.

## FIX: "Allow background activity" card never showed (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **BUG (owner: "the popup isn't showing, and not on every open even when it is off"):** in `native-android/LiquidNative.java`, method `isBackgroundAllowed`, the v210c edit left a `// v210c: ...` comment at the END of the line that also held `... call.resolve(o);`. A `//` comment runs to the end of the line, so **the resolve call was inside the comment**: it compiled fine, but the native call NEVER answered. In `src/bgallow.js`, `maybeShow()` does `await Native.isBackgroundAllowed()`, waited for ever, `bgAllowCheck()` never left its `busy` flag, and every later 1 s poll returned at once. Result: no card at start, no card on later opens, whatever Android's switch said. (`allowed = ignoring` itself was right.) The older tests could not see it: `tests/background-allowed-v210.test.mjs` only regex-matches `o.put("allowed", ignoring);`, and `tests/permchain-sim.mjs` / `bg-reopen-after-trip-v212.test.mjs` replace `Native.isBackgroundAllowed` with a JS stub that always answers.
- **FIX 1 (the cause):** `LiquidNative.java` `isBackgroundAllowed`: the comment is on its own lines, `call.resolve(o);` is on its own line, the result again carries `allowed`, `ignoring`, `restricted`, `appOpBlocked`. Logic unchanged.
- **FIX 2 (so a native call that never answers can never freeze the popup again):** `src/bgallow.js`: `withTimeout(Native.isBackgroundAllowed(), NATIVE_CHECK_MS = 4000)` (no answer = treated like "allowed" for now, asked again after 5 s via `okUntil`, so never a card by mistake); `busy` has a watchdog (`BUSY_MAX_MS = 20000`, token `busyTok`) so a hung picture download or Android call cannot block the next check.
- **TESTS:** new `tests/bg-allowed-resolves-v213.test.mjs`: (a) parses the real Java with comments removed and demands that EVERY `@PluginMethod` answers with resolve/reject (9 methods hand the call to Android/helpers and are listed in `HANDED_OFF`); (b) no `//` comment may contain a resolve/reject call; (c) runs the real `bgallow.js` on a fake clock: native says OFF -> card; says ON -> none; first call never answers -> card still comes; never answers at all -> keeps retrying, no card; OFF -> card again on every open (3 opens). Verified: 6 of its 10 tests FAIL on the old Java + old `bgallow.js` and pass now.
- **Files:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java`, `src/bgallow.js`, `tests/bg-allowed-resolves-v213.test.mjs`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `UPDATES.md`, this file.
- **DO NOT REDO:** never put code after a `//` comment on the same line (in Java a trailing comment on a statement line is fine ONLY when nothing follows it); keep `call.resolve(o);` on its own line; do not remove the 4 s limit or the busy watchdog; do not "fix" this by changing `allowed = ignoring` or the show-on-every-open logic (they were right).

## Background / notification / accessibility cards skipped after leaving from the Android page (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **BUG (reproduced by `tests/bg-reopen-after-trip-v212.test.mjs`):** `trip` (bgallow.js), `trip` (assist.js) and `permTrip` (main.js) were set when the person tapped Allow / Open and cleared by the NEXT `visibilitychange` whatever happened. A person who left from the Android page (Home) and opened the app much later had that open swallowed as "back from settings": `shownOpen` / `stage` / `permShownOpen` were not reset, so the card did not come.
- **FIX:** the trip only counts as "same open" when the person was away less than `TRIP_MAX_MS` (bgallow 10 s, notification 10 s, assist 10 min because step 2 is manual). Longer = a new open (flags reset, cards come again while the permission is still off). Files: `src/bgallow.js`, `src/assist.js`, `src/main.js`, new test.
- **FAIL-SAFE (same entry):** `bgAllowHold` (All files access request) releases after 30 s. Normal gaps between popups stay 10 s (`WAIT_MS`); the extra 8 s in `bgAllowCheck` only applies when the picture cannot be read (GitHub unreachable).
- **Live check:** `okUntil` re-check while allowed is 5 s (was 15 s); the poll itself is 1 s (`BGALLOW_POLL_MS`).
- **TESTS:** `tests/permchain-sim.mjs` (helper, not a test) runs the real assist.js + bgallow.js together per scenario; `tests/bg-reopen-after-trip-v212.test.mjs` asserts them.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not remove the time limit; do not make the trip flag permanent again.

## Colour field follows the Canvas and custom GIF / picture (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Problem:** the colour behind Now Playing / Lyrics was read from ONE static image (the cover, or the custom still picture). With a Canvas (video / GIF) on, the moving picture and the colour field could be different colours. A custom GIF was read from its first frame only.
- **Changed:** `src/main.js`. `coverTint` now asks `tintSource(a, t)` what the screen shows: Canvas (on + playable) > song picture / GIF > album picture / GIF > original cover. Canvas video = 7 frames from a hidden `<video>` (`tintVideoPx`); GIF / animated WebP / APNG (Canvas or custom song / album picture) = up to 8 frames via `ImageDecoder` (`tintDecodeFrames`), else the first frame. Frames are joined and read by `tintFromPixels` (the old analysis, moved out unchanged, only the `sm(k1) < 1.2` threshold now scales with the frame count). If the Canvas cannot be read, the picture under it is used. `npApplyTint` / `lyApplyTint` only let the newest read paint (`npTintSeq` / `lyTintSeq`). Lyrics-colour sheet text now says Auto follows what the song shows.
- **Keys:** `p.ck` (the key of the person's picked lyrics colour) is the SAME with or without Canvas, so a picked colour keeps working. Only the cache key (`key`) gets `~cv:<path>` while a Canvas shows. The 3 places that drop a changed picture's colour still use `covTint.keys()` with `startsWith(id + '|')` (tests extract them with a fake `covTint`): do not move them into a helper.
- **Tests:** new `tests/tint-follows-canvas-v208.test.mjs`; `tests/lyrics-colour-hex.test.mjs` text checks updated to the new function names / sentence.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not go back to reading the colour from one static image while a Canvas shows; do not key the lyrics colour by Canvas path; do not read the on-screen `<video>` for colour (a hidden copy is used so playback is not touched).
- **Verified:** `npm test` 728 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped; `node --check` OK. **NOT verified** on a phone: real Canvas files, `ImageDecoder` in the phone's WebView (if missing, a GIF uses its first frame).

- **Changed:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java` (`openBackgroundSettings`, the `oplusFamily()` branch only), `tests/background-power-page.test.mjs` (two assertions reversed, one regex updated).

- **How:** on the family, `ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS` (this app's App info) opens first, with the existing native toast "Tap Battery usage, then switch on Allow background activity." The direct power page (`openOplusAppPage`) is only a fallback if App info cannot open. Other brands are unchanged.
- **Not possible:** an app cannot tap the Battery usage row inside another app's settings, so the one tap stays manual.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not try the direct power page before App info on the family; do not put the generic battery page in front of App info.
- **Verified:** `npm test` 711 pass, 0 fail. **NOT verified** on a real phone.

## Background card: the OnePlus switch is now also read from the background app-ops (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java` (`isBackgroundAllowed` only), `tests/permission-cards-every-open-v208.test.mjs` (the `allowed` assertion now includes `!appOpBlocked`, plus one new test), `tests/java-probe.py` and `tests/java-probe-update.py` (stubs `android.app.AppOpsManager`, `android.os.Process`).
- **Why:** owner: the card must show on every open whenever "Allow background activity" is OFF. Android has no direct API for the OnePlus switch; the two signals used so far (battery-optimization exemption, `isBackgroundRestricted`) might both say "allowed" while the switch is off. Added a third signal: `AppOpsManager.checkOpNoThrow` for `android:run_in_background` and `android:run_any_in_background` on the app's own uid (mode 1 ignored / 2 errored = OFF). A phone with no such app-op, or that does not answer, counts as not blocked. Result now also carries `appOpBlocked`; `allowed = ignoring && !restricted && !appOpBlocked`.
- **Not confirmed on a device:** whether the OnePlus switch is stored as these app-ops is unknown. Every check writes `background: allowed check ignoring=.. restricted=.. appOpBlocked=..` to the app log (`adb logcat -s LiquidNative`): with the switch OFF at least one value must be `false` / `true` accordingly; if all three say "allowed" while the switch is OFF, the switch is not readable by a normal app and this approach needs another signal (do NOT guess OEM keys).
- **Unchanged:** the show-on-every-open logic in `src/bgallow.js` (`shownOpen`, `hiddenAt` >= 10 s, `trip`, `okUntil`).
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not remove the app-ops signal without a log showing it is wrong; do not make an unreadable answer count as OFF (that would show the card forever on phones that are fine).

## FIX: Allow on the background card landed on Android's generic "Battery usage" page (Unrestricted / Optimized / Restricted) on OnePlus (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java` only, inside `oplusAppPageScan()` (two `continue` lines; the rest of the file is byte-identical); one new test at the end of `tests/background-power-page.test.mjs`.
- **Why:** owner (OnePlus) sent a screenshot: tapping Allow opened the page titled "Battery usage" with Unrestricted / Optimized / Restricted, not the "Liquid Dark" page with POWER CONSUMPTION CONTROLS. That page is Android's OWN Settings app page (`AdvancedPowerUsageDetail`). The scan accepted any exported pre-installed page whose package name contains "settings" and whose class name contains "powerusagedetail", and `com.android.settings` matches both, so the scan opened the generic page. (Most likely cause from the code; not confirmed on a device. The app log line `background: ... opened` / `settings opened via ...` shows which page opened.)
- **How:** the scan now skips packages starting with `com.android.settings` / `com.google.android.settings` and any class name containing `advancedpowerusage`. On OnePlus / OPPO / Realme, when the protected `com.oplus.battery` page does not open, the result is again THIS app's App info + the native toast (the intended path).
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not let the scan accept Android's Settings app again; do not put the generic battery page back in front of App info on the family.

## Allow on the background card opens the "Power consumption controls" page on OnePlus / OPPO / Realme (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java` (`openBackgroundSettings` + new `oplusFamily()`, `bgStart()`, `oplusAppPageScan()`, `openOplusAppPage()`; the rest of the file is byte-identical), new `tests/background-power-page.test.mjs`; `tests/java-probe.py` and `tests/java-probe-update.py` got extra stubs (ComponentName, ActivityInfo, PackageInfo, `Log.i` / `Log.w`, `Intent.setComponent`, a `LIB_POOL` field and `eqNorm` / `eqSystemApp` stand-ins) because the extracted range now contains the new code; both probes pass.
- **Why:** owner (OnePlus): tapping Allow on the "Keep the music playing" card must open the app's own page titled "Liquid Dark" with POWER CONSUMPTION CONTROLS (Allow foreground activity, Allow background activity), PRIVACY PERMISSIONS (Allow auto launch) and Force stop. It did not: Android's generic battery page (`VIEW_ADVANCED_POWER_USAGE_DETAIL`) was tried first and stopped the search, so that page was never reached. Owner then asked for OPPO and other brands too.
- **How:** OnePlus, OPPO and Realme (`oplusFamily()`, one ColorOS / OxygenOS page): `openOplusAppPage()` first: known `PowerConsumptionActivity` classes (`com.oplus.battery`, then `com.coloros.oppoguardelf`; both names are used by public autostart libraries), else a run-time scan (`oplusAppPageScan()`) for an EXPORTED page of a PRE-INSTALLED battery / power app; the page found is remembered for the session; the package is passed as extras and as `package:` data. **Web research finding:** on newer OnePlus / ColorOS (OnePlus 8 and later) the `com.oplus.battery` pages are PROTECTED: starting them from a normal app throws `SecurityException ... requires oplus.permission.OPLUS_COMPONENT_SAFE` (AutoStarter issue #87; adding the permission to the manifest does not help). So when the direct page does not open, the family goes to THIS app's App info (`ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS`, public); its "Battery usage" row is the page in the owner's screenshot (every OnePlus / OPPO guide uses Settings > Apps > App management > app > Battery usage). That path also resolves `hint` and shows a NATIVE Android toast "Tap Battery usage, then switch on Allow background activity." (a web toast would be hidden behind Settings). The generic battery page is NOT tried before App info on the family (it was the page that landed wrong). Every other brand keeps its own order (generic battery page, then its brand page from `openOemBackgroundPage`); only when both fail the same run-time scan is tried for it, then MIUI, ignore dialog, optimization list, app info (with the same hint). Everything runs on `LIB_POOL` (the scan is slow). Every try and candidate is written to the app log as `background: ...`.
- **Not universal:** the exact screen in the owner's screenshot is the ColorOS / OxygenOS page. Samsung, Vivo, Xiaomi, Huawei and Pixel have their own equivalent pages (already routed by `openOemBackgroundPage` / the generic page); do not send them to the oplus classes.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not put the generic battery page back in front of App info on OnePlus / OPPO / Realme; do not try to get `oplus.permission.OPLUS_COMPONENT_SAFE` (not grantable to a normal app); do not show the hint as a web toast (hidden behind Settings); do not guess more class names (they differ per version, the scan finds them); do not touch the repeat-on-every-open logic (`isBackgroundAllowed`, `src/bgallow.js`): it already works.
- **Docs updated for this:** `docs/FEATURES.md` (K39), `README.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `UPDATES.md`, this file.
- **Verified:** `npm test` all pass. The new Java compiled with the JDK compiler module against hand-made stubs and 18 routing checks passed (OnePlus / OPPO / Realme: power page first even when the generic page also opens, both package names, run-time scan, non-exported / user-app pages skipped, PROTECTED pages -> App info + hint + toast with the generic page skipped; Samsung, Vivo, Pixel unchanged; other brands: run-time search fallback, then app info). **NOT verified:** a real Android compile, or any real phone. On a protected-page phone the person lands on App info and taps Battery usage once. Read the `background:` lines in the app log to see which step opened.

## Equalizer opens the right page per phone brand: OnePlus = Dirac Audio Tuner, OPPO / Realme = Real Sound (unchanged), other brands = their own page (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `native-android/LiquidNative.java` (only the equalizer region: new `eqBrand()`, `eqSystemApp()`, `diracRoute()`, `EQ_KEYS` + `effectsRoute()`, `eqPanel()`, and `openEqualizer` now picks the route by brand and resolves `{ via, brand }`), `src/native.js` (comment + new `Native.equalizerHint(brand)`), `src/main.js` (the Equalizer row text and the toast now use `Native.equalizerHint(r.brand)`), new `tests/equalizer-brand-route.test.mjs`.
- **Why:** owner: on OnePlus phones the Equalizer row must open the "Dirac Audio Tuner" screen (Smart / Movie / Game / Music), the way it opens Real Sound Technology on OPPO; other Android brands (Samsung, Vivo...) must go to whatever sound-effects page they have. OPPO must NOT change.
- **How it routes:** brand = `Build.MANUFACTURER` + `Build.BRAND` (OnePlus is tested BEFORE OPPO; Realme counts as OPPO). OPPO / Realme: `realSoundRoute()` exactly as before (that block is byte-identical to the previous build). OnePlus: `diracRoute()` finds an EXPORTED, enabled page of a PRE-INSTALLED app whose class name or label says "Dirac" (best: "Dirac Audio Tuner"), else Sound & vibration (it never opens Real Sound). Other brands: `effectsRoute()` (exported page of a pre-installed app named equalizer / sound alive / sound quality (and effects) / audio effect / sound effect / music effect / dolby), else Android's standard equalizer panel (`eqPanel()`, only when a PRE-INSTALLED app answers it), else Sound & vibration. Pages found are cached for the session (`eqRouteCache` + `eqRouteVia`); a page that stops opening clears the cache. Every candidate is written to the app log as `equalizer: candidate ...`.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not change the OPPO / Realme route; do not let OnePlus fall into Real Sound; do not open user-installed equalizer apps (system apps only, on purpose); do not guess Dirac / Samsung / Vivo class names (they differ per OxygenOS / One UI / OriginOS version; the scan finds them); keep `eqBrand()` checking OnePlus first.
- **Docs updated for this:** `README.md` (Equalizer bullet), `docs/FEATURES.md` (K21), `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `UPDATES.md`, this file.
- **Verified:** `npm test` all pass (new file `tests/equalizer-brand-route.test.mjs`, 8 tests). The new Java compiles with the JDK compiler module against hand-made stubs and 19 routing checks passed (brand detection, Dirac found / not exported / missing, OPPO unchanged, Samsung and Vivo pages, standard panel system-app-only, refusal then rescan). **NOT verified:** a real Android compile, or any real phone. The Dirac page may not be exported to other apps on some OxygenOS versions; then the person lands on Sound & vibration and taps Dirac Audio Tuner once (the toast says so). If that happens, read the `equalizer: candidate` lines in the app log for the real page name.

## Permission cards in order: notification first, background 10 s after it goes away; phone-brand settings pages (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `src/bgallow.js` (new `sequence()` runs every second: while the notification card (`#sheet .popcard`) is open it remembers that, and the moment it is gone the background card's 10 s countdown (`readyAt`) starts; also waits while the notification card is still due via `setBgAllowGate()`, at most 45 s after launch; `launchAt` resets on a new open), `src/main.js` (`permOff` + `setBgAllowGate(() => permOff && !permShownOpen)`; Allow on the notification card is wrapped in `bgAllowHold(notifAllow())` so the background card waits for Android's prompt, then 10 s), `native-android/LiquidNative.java` (`openBackgroundSettings` now tries Android's battery-usage page, then the PHONE BRAND's own page via new `openOemBackgroundPage()`: Oppo / Realme / OnePlus, Vivo / iQOO, Samsung, Huawei / Honor, Xiaomi / Redmi / Poco, Asus, Tecno / Infinix / itel; then the older generic fallbacks), `tests/java-probe.py` + `tests/java-probe-update.py` (Build stub got MANUFACTURER / BRAND), `tests/permission-cards-every-open-v208.test.mjs` (one assertion loosened to `notifAllow()`), new `tests/permission-sequence-v209.test.mjs`.
- **Why:** owner: when both permissions are off, the notification card comes first (fast), and the background card only 10 s AFTER the notification card is gone, never together; "Allow" must lead to the right settings page on any Android brand. Both cards already use their own uploaded picture folders (`notification-popup-gif-pic/`, `background-allow-gif-pic/`): unchanged.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not remove `sequence()` or the gate (the cards would stack or the countdown would start at launch again); do not start the 10 s from launch; do not merge the two picture folders; keep the brand list in `openOemBackgroundPage()` (a class that does not exist on a phone just fails quietly).
- **Docs updated for this:** `README.md` (both permission-card bullets), `docs/FEATURES.md` (K39), `background-allow-gif-pic/README.md`, `notification-popup-gif-pic/README.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `UPDATES.md`, this file.
- **Timeline test:** `tests/permission-timeline-v209.test.mjs` runs the real `bgallow.js` with a fake clock: both OFF -> notification card at +3 s, gone at +20 s -> background card never before +30 s; also checks the next-open repeat. If the background picture is unreadable (offline / slow) the card waits up to 8 s more (never less than 10 s after the notification card).
- **Not tested on a real phone** (brand activity names change between ColorOS / OriginOS / One UI versions; if none opens, the generic pages open instead). `npm test`: 690 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped.

## Allow background activity + Allow notifications cards: shown on every open while OFF (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `src/bgallow.js` (while `Native.isBackgroundAllowed()` is false the card shows once per open, with the picture from `background-allow-gif-pic/` or plain text; `shownOpen` / `trip` / `hiddenAt` / `okUntil`; a NEW upload still shows once even when allowed; plain card after the picture was unreadable for ~18 s; waits while `#sheet .popcard` is open), `src/main.js` (`startPopup()`: `wantsPerm = !granted && !permShownOpen`, plain card allowed, ~10 s grace for a slow picture; new `permShownOpen` / `permTrip` / `permHiddenAt`; `notifAllow()`; the card never opens on top of `#lf-bgallow`), `src/native.js` + `native-android/LiquidNative.java` (new `openNotificationSettings`), the two picture-folder READMEs, `README.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md` (K39, K35), `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `UPDATES.md`, new `tests/permission-cards-every-open-v208.test.mjs`.
- **Why:** owner: the two permission cards must show on every app open until the person accepts, each with its own uploaded picture (`background-allow-gif-pic/` and `notification-popup-gif-pic/`, separate).
- **Background "allowed" now needs two Android signals (OnePlus / Oppo switch):** `isBackgroundAllowed` in `LiquidNative.java` returns `allowed = ignoring && !restricted` (`PowerManager.isIgnoringBatteryOptimizations` AND `ActivityManager.isBackgroundRestricted()`, API 28+) plus the raw `ignoring` / `restricted` values. Reason: the owner's OnePlus has its own switch (Battery usage > Allow background activity, plus Allow auto launch) that Android gives no direct API for; reading only the battery-optimization flag could say "allowed" while that switch is off. Either signal off = OFF = card on every open. `tests/java-probe.py` and `tests/java-probe-update.py` got an `ActivityManager` stub + `ACTIVITY_SERVICE` so the real Java still compiles. **Known limit:** a phone whose own switch is not reflected by either signal cannot be read by any app; a phone where switching it ON leaves the app battery-optimized would keep showing the card (check on the owner's phone and tell the next AI the `ignoring` / `restricted` values).
- **A "new open"** = cold start, or the app back in the foreground after 10 s or more away. Coming back from Android's prompt / settings (`permTrip` / `trip`) is not a new open, so the card does not loop.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not gate the notification card on `lf.notifAsked` again (it is still written, but no longer decides); do not make the cards show every second; do not merge the two pictures; do not show both cards at once.
- **Not tested on a phone or with a Java build here** (the new Java method is a plain Intent call); the test file checks the code, it cannot run Android.

## Imports are written into the app's own data first, and unsaved playlists are never dropped (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `src/main.js` (`savePl()` now resolves true / false; `plImportOk()` is async and awaits the save before showing "Imported", with a failure text; `loadPlaylists()` keeps playlists that are in memory but not in the file and saves them again, so an older or empty file cannot replace them; `applyImportedSettings()` reads `lf.settings` back before the reload and refuses to reload if it was not written; `lsImportOk()` awaits the play-count save and reports a failure), new `tests/import-kept-v208.test.mjs` (7 tests), `UPDATES.md`, `HANDOFF_STATUS.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`.
- **Why:** owner: after an import everything must be in the app's own storage, independent of the import file or folder, and playlists leave only when the person deletes them.
- **Verified in code:** `openFiles` (Java) copies the picked file to a temp file, hands the TEXT to JS and deletes the temp file; nothing stores a path or URI. Playlists -> `filesDir/playlists.json` (+ `playlists.bak`), settings -> `lf.settings` / extras in localStorage, listening -> SQLite `ListenDb.importRows` (committed transaction), play counts -> the stats file. Only `del-ok` calls `playlists.splice`.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not make imports keep a reference to the picked file; do not make `loadPlaylists()` replace memory with the file again; do not show "Imported" before the save finished.
- **Not tested on a phone.**

## Imported playlists vanished after a restart: `setLibrary()` no longer wipes loaded playlists (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Symptom (owner's report):** right after importing playlists they were gone after closing the app (not in Library).
- **Cause:** `setLibrary()` in `src/data.js` did `playlists = []`. On a normal start `main.js` reads favourites / stats / playlists in parallel with the saved library; when the playlists were read first, the library arriving afterwards emptied them in memory (the file was still fine). Library then showed none, and the next `savePl()` (an import, a new playlist) wrote only the new ones over the file.
- **Changed:** `src/data.js` (`plLoaded` flag set by `setPlaylists()`; `setLibrary()` only clears the built-in DEMO playlists, i.e. when no playlists were ever loaded), new `tests/playlists-survive-v208.test.mjs` (fails on the old code, checked), `UPDATES.md`, `HANDOFF_STATUS.md`, `docs/HANDOFF.md`.
- **Looked at, unchanged:** `plImportOk()` / `savePl()` / Java `savePlaylists` (atomic temp file + `playlists.bak`), `relinkIds` (never drops a playlist), `applyImportedSettings()` (writes `lf.settings` synchronously before the reload), `lsImportOk()` / `ListenDb.importRows` (one committed transaction, stats saved after).
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not put `playlists = []` back in `setLibrary()`; do not reload playlists inside `setLibrary()`. Lost data cannot be recovered by code: the person re-imports the playlist file.
- **Not tested on a phone** (the race depends on device speed); the unit test reproduces the order.
