## Splash: no blink before the picture, shown at least 2.8 seconds (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Changed:** `index.html` (early inline script reads + decodes the saved picture from IndexedDB `lf-splash` BEFORE `src/main.js` loads; the picture starts at opacity 0 and fades in once decoded), `src/main.js` (`splashReady` reuses `window.__lfSplashP`; `splashShow` waits for `decode()` and hides after `SPLASH_MIN_MS` counted from `window.__lfSplashAt`), `src/splashpic.js` (`SPLASH_MIN_MS = 2000`), `tests/splash-v208.test.mjs` (new), `README.md`, `UPDATES.md`.
- **Why:** the black cover was in `index.html` but the picture was attached only when the big app script ran: black screen, then the picture popped in (the blink). Also the picture used to stay 1 s.
- **DO NOT UNDO:** the early script must open the DB exactly like `idb()` in `src/splashpic.js` (name `lf-splash`, version 1, store `pic`, key `current`), or the app script could find a database without its store. ## Lyrics colour: picked colours and Auto are now EXACT, no vivid boost (no version change, still 0.208.0)

## In-app update wrongly blocked as "different key" when the phone cannot read the APK's signature (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- **Symptom (owner's report):** after a logo upload the app showed "Download failed: the downloaded APK is signed with a different key than the installed app, so Android would refuse it", right after the app had updated itself fine. The workflow's own guard step printed the SAME SHA-256 for the published key and the new build key (and `Show and verify signing certificate` showed the same V2 signer), so the key was not different.
- **Cause:** `sameSigner()` in `native-android/UpdateSupport.java` (called by `verifyApk()`). On Android 9+ it returned false when the signature list read from the downloaded APK was EMPTY (the old BUG-032 rule "empty list = broken / unsigned file = refuse"). Some phones (the APK is V2-only; vendor ROMs) return no signing info from `getPackageArchiveInfo` for a perfectly good APK, so a good download was refused and the same text as a real key change was shown.
- **Changed:** `native-android/UpdateSupport.java` (`sameSigner()`: `if (sa.isEmpty()) return true;`, comment rewritten; only two READABLE, DIFFERENT certificates now return false). `tests/update-java-harness.mjs` (helper only: the `Signature` stub keeps a text, the `SigningInfo` stub has a settable certificate history, the driver's fake PackageManager can return a certificate for the installed app and for the archive, new driver command `verify28` = Android 9+ with a 1.1 MB file). New `tests/same-signer-unreadable.test.mjs` (6 tests: unreadable APK signature accepted, same certificate accepted, two different readable certificates still refused, unreadable own certificate accepted, build-number reasons still reported, source check). Docs: this entry, `docs/HANDOFF.md`, `UPDATES.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md` (K27).
- **Second chance (same entry):** `sameSigner()` also asks again with the old `GET_SIGNATURES` flag (Android 9+) when the new signing list is empty, for the APK and for the installed app, because some phones fill only that list. This makes a real mismatch still get caught and "unreadable" rarer. Tests: 3 more in `tests/same-signer-unreadable.test.mjs` (old list same = accepted, old list different = refused, nothing readable = accepted); the harness takes two optional extra args for the old-flag certificates.
- **Why it is safe:** the update is still bound to our exact APK by the manifest's SHA-256 and exact build number (both checked before the signer check), and Android's own installer refuses a really different signing key, so nothing is lost by letting it decide when the signature cannot be read.
- **`.github/workflows/android.yml`: NOT changed.** Its key guard compares the published and the new key correctly (`published key SHA-256` / `new build key SHA-256` lines in the apk job). Do not "fix" the workflow for this symptom.
- **Phones that already have the old app:** the old check is in the installed app, so such a phone cannot take this build through the in-app update. Install `liquid-dark.apk` from Releases > Latest build once by hand over the old app (library and settings stay). In-app updates work normally after that.
- **DO NOT REDO:** do not bring back "empty APK signature on Android 9+ = refuse". Do not treat an unreadable signature as a key change. Do not remove the SHA-256 / build checks that make this safe. Keep the real-mismatch case (two readable, different certificates) refused; `tests/same-signer-unreadable.test.mjs` covers both directions.
- **Checked:** the new tests run against the real `UpdateSupport.java` compiled with the Java stubs (6 pass; against the OLD java the unreadable-signature test fails, so the test really reproduces the bug). Not tested on a phone.

## Settings maximums raised; the physical limits are raised with them, not removed (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- WHAT: every Settings > Animation slider now goes higher: pill wobble up/down and left/right 200 (was 110), liquid stretch 200 (was 130), pill size 150 (was 110), magnification 200 (was 120), bounciness 200 (was 140), spring speed 200 (was 130), button squash and jiggle 200 (was 110), toggle knob fluidity 200 (was 110), glass edge fringe 250 (was 150). The minimums are unchanged.
- THE CEILINGS THAT ACTUALLY LIMIT THE LOOK WERE RAISED TOGETHER (so the sliders do something above 100 %): `EDGE_WOBBLE_MAX` 1.6 and `MAGNIFY_MAX` 1.6 in `src/animcfg.js` (were 1.2 and 1.4, used in `src/glidebar.js`; the tab-bar icons still have their own fit check); button squash clamp 0.90-1.12 in `src/jelly.js` (was 0.92-1.08); CSS wobble `lqAmp` 1.3 (was 1.0, in `GLASS_LIMITS`); fringe ceiling 3 px (was 1.8); toggle knob grow 6 px (was 4) and height 22-30 px (was 22-29), so the knob still leaves 1 px on each side of the 32 px track.
- STAYS CAPPED ON PURPOSE: the spring overshoot for sheets, screens and the knob (`springOver` 1.45, `liqOver` 1.35), the durations (0.35-1.4 s, dur-k 0.5-2) and the stability of the springs (speed 200 is the highest; higher would make the springs unstable at 60 Hz). Bounciness above 100 lowers the spring damping, so the JS springs jiggle more, but no CSS overshoot goes beyond the caps.
- TESTED: `python3 tests/glass_extremes.py` at the new tops (every slider at its top, and two mixed corners, Watery, min, Max preset): no toggle knob outside its track, no lens outside its bar, no text overflow, nothing off screen, no JS errors. Worst scale on any control 1.12 (the new squash ceiling; was 1.044). The audit now fails if any control scales past 1.15. `npm test`: 633 pass on Node 22 and 24.
- FILES: `src/animcfg.js` (ANIM_DEFS maxima, GLASS_LIMITS, EDGE_WOBBLE_MAX, MAGNIFY_MAX), `src/glidebar.js` (uses the two ceilings), `src/jelly.js` (squash clamp), `src/liquidui.js` (knob clamps), `tests/glass-settings-v208.test.mjs` (new maxima), `tests/glass_extremes.py` (new maxima, new "top of every slider" case, scale threshold).
- DO NOT REDO: do not raise speed above 200 (the spring would become unstable at 60 Hz). Do not raise `springOver` or `liqOver` without checking the sheets and the toggle knob on a phone. Any new ceiling must have its own fit check.
- NOT verified: the feel on a phone at the new tops; the audit is in Chromium only.

- WHAT: (1) every liquid glass CSS value that Settings > Animation drives (`--lq-amp`, `--lq-dur`, `--dur-k`, `--spring`, `--liq`, `--fringe`) is now made by one pure function, `glassVars()` in `src/animcfg.js`, with hard limits (`GLASS_LIMITS`): spring overshoot 1.45 (was up to 1.63, which could fling sheets and screens off their edge), toggle-knob spring 1.35, lens fringe 1.8 px, durations 0.35-1.4 s. `applySettings` in `src/main.js` writes those values. At the default settings the values are identical to before; only the extremes are held back. (2) The import sheet names the groups plainly: "Liquid glass & wobble (animation studio)", "Lyrics animation", "Lens refraction (tab bar)" instead of the raw "Anim", "Ly Anim", "Refract" (`GROUP_LABELS` + `groupLabel()` in `src/settingsio.js`). The values were already in the export (`anim`, `lyAnim`, `refract` live in the settings object); the names made them hard to find.
- FILES: `src/animcfg.js` (new `GLASS_LIMITS`, `glassVars()`), `src/main.js` (`applySettings` uses `glassVars`; import sheet uses `SIO.groupLabel`; `glassVars` imported), `src/settingsio.js` (`GROUP_LABELS`, `groupLabel`), `tests/glass-settings-v208.test.mjs` (new, 8 tests), `tests/glass-settings.test.mjs` (two guards now read `glassVars` in `animcfg.js` instead of inline strings in `main.js`), `tests/glass_extremes.py` (new browser audit), `package.json` (script `glass-extremes`).

Lens fringe applies to the lens edges (tab bar, button-row pills) by design; resting buttons have no fringe.

## Live RAM + CPU threads, usage only (no version change, still 0.208.0)
- WHAT: Settings > Performance: "Memory (RAM)" shows only the current used RAM (e.g. "4.9 GB"); the "CPU cores" row is now "CPU threads" and shows the live CPU usage of the app plus its thread count (e.g. "2.3% · 23 threads"). Both refresh every 1000 ms while Settings is on top (only the two texts change). `src/main.js` (`ramText`, `cpuUpdate`, `cpuText`, the 1000 ms poll); `native-android/LiquidNative.java` `getDeviceInfo` also returns `cpuMs` (`Process.getElapsedCpuTime()`) and `threads` (`/proc/self/task`). The % = growth of the app's CPU time per second / cores (Android blocks reading the whole phone's CPU from /proc/stat for apps since 8.0, so it is this app's usage). Replaces the previous "X used of Y" RAM text.
- NOT verified: Java not compiled, not run on a phone; JS checked in Chromium with a faked bridge. In a plain browser the CPU row says "Not available here".

## Workflow: test job on Node 24, Playwright 1.63.0, skipped-check works on Node 22 and 24 (no version change, still 0.208.0)

## App logo is baked into the APK again, so a logo upload really changes what users get (no version change, still 0.148.0)
- PROBLEM (reported): dropping a picture into `app-logo-pic/` changed the fingerprint and offered an update, but the update delivered nothing. Since v144 the APK has no launcher icon at all (`scripts/no-launcher-icon.py` strips it), so installing the "new logo" build changed no icon on any phone. The fingerprint -> build -> manifest -> update chain itself was simulated step by step in a scratch Git repo and works; the missing piece was that nothing in the build used the picture.
- FIXED: new `scripts/apply-app-logo.py`. It reads `app-logo-pic/latest.json`, loads the named picture (PNG / JPG / WEBP / BMP / GIF, EXIF-rotated, GIF = first non-blank frame) and writes legacy square + round icons (48-192 px), Android 8+ adaptive foreground/background layers (108-432 px, picture inside the safe zone over a blurred copy of itself, dark background for transparent pictures) and `mipmap-anydpi-v26/ic_launcher*.xml`, deletes template icons in other formats (duplicate-resource guard) and sets `android:icon` / `android:roundIcon` on `<application>` only (comment-safe).
- WORKFLOW (`android.yml`, apk job): the step "No launcher icon" is now "Launcher icon from app-logo-pic/". Exit 0 = icon applied; exit 3 = no usable logo, falls back to `scripts/no-launcher-icon.py` (a bad picture prints a `::warning::` and a run-summary line, never silent); anything else fails the build. "Verify APK package" now also checks with `aapt dump badging` that the finished APK has a launcher icon when one was applied.
- FINGERPRINT (`scripts/app-fingerprint.py`): now also hashes the exact picture `latest.json` names (the bytes that go into the icon), not only `latest.json`. Other pictures in the folder do not change it. Same selection rule in both scripts: no `latest.json` = no logo.
- UNCHANGED: live About logo (`src/applogo.js`, 1000 ms), the resend marker / Run workflow tick, the six live artwork folders, version number, signing guard.
- DO NOT REDO: do not put a bundled default logo back in `public/`; do not make the fingerprint depend on every file in `app-logo-pic/`.
- Tests: new `tests/apply-app-logo.test.mjs` (sizes, adaptive xml, manifest edit with a decoy comment, every picture type, blank-first-frame GIF, transparent PNG, no/invalid/traversal/corrupt logo = exit 3, broken project = exit 2); `tests/fingerprint.test.mjs` now covers stamp, bytes and selection; `tests/logo-notification-workflow.test.mjs` checks the new step order. Needs Pillow (the workflow test job already installs it).
- NOT verified: a real Gradle build, the adaptive icon on a phone, launcher icon refresh after an in-place update, and the aapt badging check against a real APK. First real run: upload a logo, check the run summary says "Launcher icon: built from ...", then install the update.

Now `picsMissing()` / `reloadMissingPics()` include them (15 s watch, 2.5 s / 6 s start retries, every resume), using tiny marks `lf.hasbg` / `lf.haspf` (set when saved or restored, cleared on remove) plus the file list. | WHERE: `src/main.js` `bgSaved`, `pfSaved`, `setFlag`, `hasFlag`.

## Pictures survive a restart; one picture per thing, same everywhere (no version change, still 0.148.0)

- FIXED (picture bug): `artistKey` stripped ALL combining marks, which are vowel signs / voiced marks in Hindi, Tamil, Telugu, Thai, Arabic, Hebrew and Japanese (dakuten). Latin accents / "The" / punctuation still merge as before.

- FIXED (compatibility): listening history of songs that are NOT in the library (Tempo / Last.fm / Spotify imports, deleted songs) showed each raw tag as an artist ("Sean Paul, Keyshia Cole", "The Weeknd feat. Ed Sheeran", "Weeknd" next to "The Weeknd"), ignoring Smart artist grouping | WHERE: `src/data.js` `snapshotArtists` + `learnArtists` (same split / merge / shown-spelling rules as the library; history teaches which names are single artists; a credit list led by the album artist is split; protected acts like "Tyler, The Creator" stay whole; grouping OFF = tag as it is), `src/lstats.js` `meta` (unmatched plays) + `fetchSegs`, `native-android/ListenDb.java` `seen()` also returns the title. The STORED rows are never changed (grouping is applied when reading), so switching grouping on / off, importing and exporting stay lossless.
- IMPROVED Tempo import (`src/listensrc.js` `fromTempo`, checked on the owner's real file, 4144 events): dropped junk "songs" Tempo scraped from notifications (library scan text, social notifications, ads, "N views"; `isJunkSong`); "artist \u2022 Recommended for you" cleaned; swapped file-name fields fixed; the track's PRIMARY artist is the album artist; the time zone of each play (`tzOffsetMinutes`) is kept (`play().tz`, `planPlays`); one play seen by two apps counts once. Tempo's `sessionId` is ONE PER PLAY (4144 sessions for 4144 plays), so it is NOT used: our 30 minute rule builds sessions. Play ids are still made from the RAW artist + title (`rawArtist` / `rawTitle`), so re-importing a file an older version imported adds nothing twice. Result on the real file: 4140 plays, 4036 count (was 4144 / 4039).
- IMPROVED Tempo export: same top-level keys / defaults as the real 4.8.15 backup (full `userPreferences`, the 28 built-in badges as not earned, `albums`, PRIMARY / FEATURED credits split on commas and "feat.", `volumeLevel`, real `tzOffsetMinutes`, `duration`, the user's name). Still NOT tested inside Tempo.

## Listening Snapshot: shareable picture for day / week / month / year / all time (no version change, still 0.148.0)

A preview sheet offers Save image (system "save as" dialog) and Share (Android share sheet; browser: Web Share, else download). Works with ANY amount of data (one song heard for 10 seconds gives a full card); a period with no listening says so instead of making an empty card.

Covers are the ORIGINAL covers (like every stats screen); a missing / unreadable cover is drawn as a colour gradient; a canvas the browser will not export is redrawn without covers.

## v148 signing-secret guard (no version change, still 0.148.0)

It fails the run when such a file is tracked in the repo or is inside an uploaded zip. **Paste the new `android.yml` by hand** (a zip never replaces `.github/`). No app-fingerprint input changed, so no new APK is built.

## v148 bug-check fixes (version 0.148.0)
- Widgets: "Top artist" now uses the same artist rules as the app where the library is not needed (`A; B`, `A feat. B`, `A (feat. B)`, `feat.` names in the song title, then the album artist). Splitting `A, B` / `A & B` needs the library, so those tags stay one name in a widget. `StatsWidgets.artistsOf`.
- Widgets: resizing a widget no longer reads the database on the main thread (`onAppWidgetOptionsChanged` uses `goAsync()` + a thread, like `onUpdate`).
- Widgets: one not-exact, non-wake-up alarm just after local midnight redraws all stats widgets, so "Today / This week / This month" roll over on time (before: only Android's ~30 minute timer). Scheduled from `onUpdate`, `onEnabled` and the alarm itself; it stops re-arming when no stats widget is left. `ACTION_MIDNIGHT`, request code 31.
- Backup restore: the Liquid backup now carries `tz` (the zone each listen happened in; `ListenDb.query` returns it), and `planLiquid` keeps it. Old backups without `tz` use this phone's zone, as before.
- `lstats.js`: a failed history read is not retried in a tight render loop (5 s back-off per period; a forced reload still goes through).
- Import toast says "listening records" (it counts stored stretches, not plays). `parseTime`: removed a no-op expression. Repeat-one: the last stretch of a repeated song now ends at the song's end position, not about 0 (`ListenTracker`, `MEDIA_ITEM_TRANSITION_REASON_REPEAT`).
- New test in `tests/listenio.test.mjs`; `npm test` 290/290 (293 with the key-guard tests). `android.yml`, the Bluetooth / earphone button code and everything else are unchanged. `src/` and `native-android/` are fingerprint inputs, so this builds a new APK.
- NOT verified: a real phone, GitHub Actions, a full Android compile (the Java parses with the JDK compiler; only the missing Android libraries are reported), `vite build`.

## v146 update-gate tidy-up (no version change, still 0.146.0)
- `src/main.js` `updGateRestore`: when the saved update lock is junk (no build number) or its update is already installed, the saved record is now deleted from the persistent store too (before, only the old localStorage copy was removed). It could not re-lock anyone before (it was re-judged and released on every start); this just stops the stale record from lingering. A build with no code id still leaves the record alone.
- New `tests/update-gate-cleanup.test.mjs`. `src/` is part of the app fingerprint, so this is a new APK and phones are offered it once as a normal update.

Update popup only polls while an update is pending (by design); background-allow only runs in the native app and waits 10 s after launch/resume.

## v145 backup & transfer (version still 0.144.0)
Settings > Backup & transfer: playlists, listening data and settings can each be exported and imported.
- **Playlists out:** Liquid backup (.json), M3U8, XSPF (several playlists = one .zip), CSV (Exportify-style columns). A single playlist: playlist menu > Export this playlist. **In:** our JSON, M3U / M3U8, PLS, XSPF, JSPF, CSV / TSV (Exportify, Soundiiz, Apple Music text export), Spotify's Playlist1.json, plain "Artist - Title" lines; several files at once. Matching: path, tags, path tail (other phone / PC), then title + first artist ignoring (feat.) / remaster tags. Songs not on the phone are listed in a sheet. Code: `src/plforeign.js`, `src/transfer.js`.
- **Listening data out:** Liquid backup (lossless), ListenBrainz JSON, Spotify-style streaming-history JSON, CSV (the last three hold only qualified plays). **In:** those plus Spotify StreamingHistory, Last.fm / Pano / Apple Music play-activity / any CSV with artist, track, date columns. Rows go into `listening.db` through `ListenDb.importRows` (INSERT OR IGNORE on UNIQUE(play_id, seq), so importing a file twice adds nothing); new qualified plays of library songs also bump Recently / Most Played. Other apps carry no listening time: the song length (or 3 min) is used. Code: `src/listenio.js`. `ListenDb.query` now also returns `n` (seq).
- **Settings:** one file with the whole `lf.settings` object (+ `lf.canvasOn`), search history left out; import asks first, applies, reloads. Pictures / GIFs picked in the app are device files and are not included. Code: `src/settingsio.js`.
- **Native:** new `saveFile`, `openFiles`, `importListens` in `LiquidNative.java` (old `exportPlaylists` / `importPlaylists` kept). `tests/smoke.py` step 8 now goes through the format sheet.
- **Verified:** `npm test` 283/283 (new: transfer, plforeign, listenio, settingsio), `node --check` on `src/`. **NOT verified:** Java never compiled (no javac here), no `vite build`, no browser or phone run. File formats are written from the public specs as I know them and were not tried against Spotify / Apple Music / ListenBrainz / Last.fm themselves.
## v144 update box: picture first (no version change, still 0.144.0)

The update is mandatory, so the wait is capped at 20 s; then the plain box opens and a late picture is swapped in. The old separate 20 s wait before opening was removed so the two waits never add up.
- New checks: `tests/update-first-draw.test.mjs` (source guard) and `tests/update-first-draw-probe.py` (browser probe: with a 3 s picture the box appears at about 3.6 s already holding the picture; with a 25 s picture the plain box opens at about 20 s).

## v144 update-install fix (no version change, still 0.144.0)
- In-app Install no longer fails: `scripts/install-native.sh` now writes `res/xml/file_paths.xml` with `files-path update/` (Capacitor's own FileProvider already exists, so the old extra `lf_file_paths.xml` was never used and `FileProvider.getUriForFile` threw for `files/update/`).
- The unverified legacy `installUpdate()` (LiquidNative.java and src/native.js) was removed; only the checksum-verified UpdateDownloadService/UpdateInstallActivity path installs APKs.
- Workflow: the apk job now needs the test job, and a new step "Verify the update installer file path (FileProvider)" fails the build if the path or provider is missing. New test: `tests/update-fileprovider.test.mjs`.

## v144 follow-up fixes from the bug report (no version change, still 0.144.0)
- Track ids vs. re-picked folder: ids are still a hash of the document URI, but `src/idrelink.js` now keeps each saved id's folder-relative path in store `idkeys` and, after every library load (`relinkSaved()` in `main.js`), moves favorites, play counts and playlist entries from dead ids to the one song with the same path (exact path, or a unique path that differs only by the picked folder level; ambiguous = untouched; empty library = untouched). Saved data made before this fix has no stored path yet, so only future re-picks are repaired.
- Scan threads: `scanThreads()` no longer forces at least 8 workers; the heap budget (about 32 MB per worker) always wins, floor 1. Heaps under 256 MB now get fewer than 8 workers. Checked by `tests/scan-threads.test.mjs`.
- Background play counts: new `native-android/PlayCounter.java` (same rules as `makeCounter()`) (STALE, BUG-282: PlayCounter has no runtime caller, ListenTracker counts plays) runs in the plugin ticker and keeps counted plays in SharedPreferences (`plays`, newest 500). The page reads them with `takePlays()` on start, when the app comes back to the foreground and on `visibilitychange`, and `mergePlays()` (stats.js) skips plays it already counted itself. Clearing play history also drains the native list. Needs a real-device check: screen off, 35+ seconds of a song, reopen the app.
- Java probes: `tests/java-probe.py` (stubs for Context, Build, Settings, Intent, FileProvider, UpdateSupport, UpdateDownloadService) and `tests/java-probe-scan.py` (THREAD_PRIORITY_FOREGROUND, LiquidTiles, the real `scanThreads()`) compile again; `tests/java-probe-update.py` falls back to the JDK compiler module when there is no `javac` binary; new `tests/java-probe-plays.py` runs `PlayCounter` for real.
- Docs: `docs/HANDOFF.md` now identifies v144 / 0.144.0 and says progress events come every 150 ms; this file and `UPDATES.md` / `README.md` no longer describe a bundled logo or launcher icon.

## v144: logo integrity, artwork retry backoff, notification delivery and signing-key guard. Version 0.144.0.
- v144 blink fix (no version change, still 0.144.0): the notification card was identified by its picture's blob URL, which changes on every poll, so an identical card was torn down and rebuilt each second (the blink). `notifSheet` now keys the card by the picture id plus button set (`data-sig`), returns whether it rebuilt, and the duplicate blob is released. The update card already patches in place. Check with `tests/blink_card_check.py` (original: 4 rebuilds in 5 s; fixed: 0) and `tests/blink_probe.py` (native emulation).
- v144 addendum (SUPERSEDED, BUG-281: the workflow DOES run scripts/apply-app-logo.py when a logo is uploaded; no-launcher-icon.py is only the fallback) (no version change): NO logo is bundled. `public/lf-app-logo-*.png` and `scripts/apply-app-logo.py` are removed; the workflow runs `scripts/no-launcher-icon.py` to strip `android:icon`/`roundIcon` from the manifest, so Android shows its generic icon. `src/applogo.js` polls `app-logo-pic/latest.json` every 1000 ms (`APP_LOGO_POLL_MS`) and shows the newest logo in Settings > About (hidden when none). `app-logo-pic/latest.json` IS in the fingerprint, so every logo upload/commit builds a new APK and offers the update (the live check runs too).
- Logo selection: the unpack job picks the newest image in `app-logo-pic/` (by Git commit order, then file name) and writes `app-logo-pic/latest.json`. `scripts/no-launcher-icon.py` only edits the `<application>` element of the manifest and ignores XML comments. `splash-pic/` remains a separate live opening-screen asset.
- Splash/Credits/update-popup-gif-pic media failures are recorded per stamp and exponentially backed off; their manifest polls remain 1000 ms. Notification delivery waits while an update is pending and verifies the real sheet image remains present through a two-frame paint window before saving its seen stamp.
- The legacy base64 logo cache (`lf.appLogo`) is deleted on startup. The mandatory update gate is persisted atomically in the native `filesDir` JSON store (`updgate.json`) and imports the old `lf.updGate` localStorage value once; a write failure is shown to the user.
- Signing guard now runs for all APK branch builds. Missing published branch/release/asset/certificate data blocks publication by default; the only bypass is the explicit `allow_new_signing_key` dispatch input (which warns that users may need to uninstall). Unknown installed build metadata is treated as unknown, not as permission to offer any different fingerprint.
- Tests for this version: live-media retry backoff, logo/splash separation, the no-launcher-icon manifest edit and the workflow/notification checks (`tests/logo-notification-workflow.test.mjs`). The EXIF / blank-frame / fallback-icon tests of the old bundled-logo implementation were removed with `scripts/apply-app-logo.py`.
- Validation in this workspace: `npm test` passes (434/434 tests, after the follow-up fixes above; updated in fix session 13). This workspace does not include installed Vite/Android SDK/Gradle dependencies or signing secrets, so a full APK build, GitHub Actions release and physical-device test are not claimed.

## v142: background update service and recovery hardening. Version 0.142.0.
- `native-android/UpdateDownloadService.java`: Android foreground `dataSync` service, progress notification, completion notification with direct Install action, serialized worker, cancellation when a newer manifest arrives, HTTP/error reporting, and sticky service recovery. Task removal does not stop the transfer.
- `native-android/UpdateSupport.java`: per-build+SHA temp/final file names in app-private files, persisted target/status/error/progress, and reusable APK/package/build/SHA verification. `UpdateInstallActivity.java` implements the notification's Install action and verifies the file before opening Android's package installer.
- `LiquidNative.java` exposes `getUpdateStatus`; install revalidates the file and invalidates missing/corrupt APK state. `src/main.js` no longer trusts the in-memory `updReady` flag, displays failure details and a Retry action, and automatically downloads again when install-time verification finds a bad/missing file.
- `scripts/install-native.sh` registers the service/activity and Android 14+ `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_DATA_SYNC` permission; FileProvider now exposes the persistent files directory. Workflow uses the supplied v141 signing-key guard and builds the new Java classes through the existing native-install step.
- The manifest polling cadence is unchanged: `src/update.js` still exports `UPDATE_POLL_MS = 1000`.
- Validation status: source/unit tests and packaging checks are run in the current workspace. A real Android Gradle build, GitHub Actions publish, and physical-device background/notification/install test require the repository signing secrets and an Android device; those are not available here.

## v141: update-chain hardening + consistent "liquid-dark" file names. Version 0.141.0.
- `.github/workflows/android.yml`: the release alias `liquid-flac.apk` and the release notes are now written AFTER `update/latest.json` is committed (new step "Update the release alias and notes", gated on the manifest step's `published` output). The apk job prints a notice if the branch moved after the plan job.
- `src/main.js`: `updGateRestore` drops a saved `lf.updGate` that has no target build (old format); the next online check re-creates it only for a genuinely newer manifest. About shows `code <fingerprint>` so the installed id can be compared with `update/latest.json`.
- Renames: release assets are `liquid-dark.apk` and `liquid-dark-<code>-<vcode>.apk`; the signing file is `signing/liquid-dark.keystore` (same key, alias `liquid dark`, same secrets). A legacy copy `liquid-flac.apk` is still uploaded for phones with an older updater. Package id `com.liquid.flacplayer` is unchanged on purpose (changing it would make Android treat the app as a different app).
- Logo uploads: a new logo changes `app-logo-pic/latest.json`, so the fingerprint changes and ONE normal update is offered. The version label may stay the same; Settings > About shows `code <fingerprint>` to tell builds apart. After installing, the baked-in id equals the manifest, so it does not repeat.
- Fingerprint design unchanged: version number is informational; live artwork folders do not rebuild; only `app-logo-pic/latest.json` does.

## v140: fingerprint-based updates and repeat-install loop fix. Version 0.140.0.
- `scripts/app-fingerprint.py` is the single app identity function. It hashes tracked APK inputs (including `app-logo-pic/latest.json`) and excludes the live image folders `notification-popup-gif-pic/`, `update-popup-gif-pic/`, `splash-pic/` and `credit-gif-pic/`. The package version is descriptive metadata, not the update identity: source changes still change the fingerprint when the version label stays at 0.140.0.
- The workflow `plan` compares the fingerprint to `update/latest.json`, the manifest committed after a successful release upload. It no longer relies on mutable release notes as the “already published” gate. APK jobs are serialized, assign a strictly rising Android `versionCode`, publish an immutable `liquid-flac-<fingerprint>-<versionCode>.apk` plus SHA-256, and then update the manifest. The familiar `liquid-flac.apk` alias remains for manual download / legacy API fallback.
- The workflow verifies the built APK's package name, `versionName` and `versionCode` before publishing. The native updater checks the downloaded APK's SHA-256, package id and exact target `versionCode` before enabling Install; the installer checks the archive again before opening Android's installer.
- `src/main.js` now checks the actual installed Android build and baked-in fingerprint before restoring/drawing a saved mandatory-update screen. If either confirms the target update is installed, `lf.updGate` is removed. A different source fingerprint with a higher build number is a new update; same fingerprint or an already-installed build is never re-offered.
- Workflow now really creates/updates/deletes `credit-gif-pic/latest.json` (previously the app polled this manifest but the workflow had no writer). All five artwork folders accept GIF, PNG, JPG/JPEG, WebP and BMP. `app-logo-pic/` changes trigger a build because launcher art is baked into Android; the other four artwork folders are live-only and do not trigger APK installs.
- Tests added for fingerprint behavior and Credits artwork handling. Validation in this workspace: Node unit tests, YAML parse, embedded Bash syntax, and Java update-method probe. A full Android Gradle build, actual GitHub Actions run and physical-device install test are not available here; signing secrets and the target repository are not accessible from this workspace.

## v139: first attempt at update-loop handling. Version 0.139.0.
- v139 added the Android `versionCode` comparison, but the update publication identity was still split between the release notes and `update/latest.json`, and the fixed APK asset name could allow stale-asset/manifest mismatches. It also did not verify the downloaded APK against the manifest before offering Install. v140 completes this by making the manifest the publication source of truth and using immutable, checksummed APK assets.

## v128 - one-second remote update checks (version 0.128.0)
- App updates, app-logo-pic manifest checks, and in-app popup manifest checks use a shared documented cadence of 1000 ms (one second) while the app is visible.
- Popup and app-logo-pic manifest cache-buster query values now roll every second as well, avoiding a 15-second cache-key window.
- Existing immediate checks on app startup, app resume, and network reconnect remain. The popup remains the existing in-app picture modal with its current Allow / Not now behavior; no Android notification-tray feature was added.
- One-millisecond polling was deliberately not used: that would mean up to 1000 polls per second per check per device and would overwhelm network/API resources. JavaScript timers are best-effort and can be throttled by Android in the background; foreground checks are approximately once per second.

## v127 - in-app popup resume monitoring (version 0.127.0)

- Added an explicit Capacitor `appStateChange` resume hook to force-check the popup manifest and remote app-logo-pic manifest. This supplements visibilitychange and online events.
- App-logo polling remains independent at 15 seconds in `src/applogo.js`; popup polling remains independent at 15 seconds in `src/main.js`.
- Not verified on a physical Android device; requires a GitHub Actions build and device test.

## v123 - popup on every send
Cause: the app marked a picture "seen" by its bytes only (`lf.popupSeen` = hash), so the same picture sent again never showed. Fix: `notification-popup-gif-pic/latest.json` = {name, commit, stamp}; the workflow writes a new stamp at every upload (picture in the repo root, same bytes too), keeps it on runs with nothing new; the app's seen id = hash + stamp (`pic.id`); a visible app polls the manifest every 60 s (`peekManifest`) and starts the popup on a new stamp. Tests in `tests/popup.test.mjs` (167 pass). Workflow step simulated in a scratch git repo (new picture, code push, same picture again, same name again, direct upload to notification-popup-gif-pic/, nothing new). Not run on a phone.

## v119 part 2: earphone buttons checked against the three manuals + play from a button when the app is closed (still version 0.119.0)
- Manuals read: TRIGGR Kraken X1, TRIGGR Ultrabuds N5 (both Bluetooth touch earbuds), boAt BassHeads 225 (wired; its product page lists no button functions; the user then gave the boAt text: one Multifunction Button, 1 press = play / pause, 2 = next, 3 = previous, long press 1.5 s = voice assistant, hold 2 s = reject call; the last two are phone functions).
- What the app can receive (full table in the comment above `keyN` in `PlaybackService.java`): Kraken + N5 work out the gesture in the earbud and send ONE Bluetooth media key: 1 tap = PLAY / PAUSE, double tap right = NEXT, double tap left = PREVIOUS. Triple tap = phone volume (the phone changes the volume, no app code). Voice assistant (Kraken: hold left 2 s, N5: 4 taps), call answer / end / reject, Gaming / Game mode and power are handled by the phone or the earbud and are never sent to a music app, so no code can react to them. BassHeads 225: HEADSETHOOK (or PLAY_PAUSE through a USB-C adapter) per press, counted inside 400 ms.
- Changed in `PlaybackService.java`: (1) play / pause uses `getPlayWhenReady()` (a buffering song no longer counts as "paused"); (2) key-up / auto-repeat events of FAST_FORWARD and REWIND are consumed like the others (no double action); (3) NEXT / PREVIOUS are ignored when the queue is empty; (4) NEW: the queue is saved to `filesDir/resume.json` (up to 1000 songs from just before the current one, current index, position, repeat) 0.8 s after a song change, a pause or a queue edit, on a helper thread (temp file + rename), and a PLAY / PLAY_PAUSE button on an EMPTY player puts it back (`resumeFromDisk`) and plays. If the app is then opened, `Native.restore` sees `alive: true` and adopts the service queue (all ids known) as before; (5) toasts now say what the button did ("Earphones: next song").
- `scripts/install-native.sh`: adds `androidx.media3.session.MediaButtonReceiver` (MEDIA_BUTTON) to the manifest, idempotent, so the system can start the service from a button press after the app was swiped away.
- Checked here: `tests/java-probe-earphones.py` compiles `PlaybackService.java` against hand-made stubs and runs 14 button cases (wired 1 / 2 / 3 presses, adapter PLAY_PAUSE, Bluetooth PLAY / PAUSE / NEXT / PREVIOUS / FF / REWIND, key-up ignored, empty queue) = all pass. `npm test` 159 pass. NOT checked: real Android / Media3 1.3.1 signatures (stubs are from memory), the resume.json write + read (no org.json here), the MediaButtonReceiver start path, anything on a phone. If the GitHub Actions build fails, look at `PlaybackService.java` (imports + `saveResume` / `resumeFromDisk`) first.
- Known limit: if the system starts the service from a button and there is no resume.json (nothing was ever played), nothing starts and the toast says so; Android 12+ may complain that no foreground notification appeared. The toast test build is still in; remove `keyMsg` when the buttons are confirmed to work.

## v119: calm Home + See All on every row. Version 0.119.0.
- See All now on EVERY Home row. New sub-screens in `src/main.js` `sub`: `mixAll` (id = daily / favmix / newmix / because / forgot; song list with Play / Shuffle) and `albHome` (id = heavy / discover / added; album list). Opened through new `lib` keys `mix:*` and `alb:*`. Lists come from `snap` (`snap.heavy`, `snap.fresh`, `snap.added` = album ids; daily picks now 30, row shows the cards-per-row count). Pinned rows say "See All" instead of "Open".
- Look: "See All" is plain accent text (class `seeall`), same title size and spacing for every row, Browse by Genre is one scrolling row (CSS block "v119: calm Home" at the end of `app.css`).
- Calm default (`src/homecfg.js`): SECTIONS reordered; visible by default = Jump back in, Playlists, Recommended Today, Recently Played, Your Top Artists, Recently Added, Browse by Genre. Everything else is off (`DEFAULT_HIDDEN`), turn on in Settings > Home screen. Quick buttons: Favorites / Most played hidden, library counts line off. `cleanHome` stamps `lv: 2`; an old Home that was never customised (hidden = ['mystats'] or missing) is moved to the new default once; customised Homes are untouched.
- Earphone / headset buttons (wired + Bluetooth): `PlaybackService` `MediaSession.Callback.onMediaButtonEvent` handles HEADSETHOOK / PLAY_PAUSE by counting presses within 400 ms (1 = play/pause, 2 = next, 3 = previous) and PLAY, PAUSE, STOP, NEXT, PREVIOUS, FAST_FORWARD, REWIND directly (Bluetooth earphones send these). Every key is logged as `earphone key ...` (Log.w, so it shows in Settings > Copy log): if no such line appears after a button press, the phone / adapter never sent it to the app. Service manifest also gets a MEDIA_BUTTON intent-filter (`scripts/install-native.sh`). Models checked in research: boAt BassHeads 225 (wired, 3.5 mm, ONE multi-function button: 1 = play/pause, 2 = next, 3 = previous; the Reno 7 has no jack so it runs through a USB-C adapter, which sends PLAY_PAUSE or HEADSETHOOK), TRIGGR Ultrabuds N5 (touch: 1 tap play/pause, double tap right = next, double tap left = previous, triple tap = volume), TRIGGR Kraken X1 (user manual checked: touch, 1 tap either earbud = play/pause, double tap right = next, double tap left = previous, triple tap right / left = volume up / down, SBC + AAC; same as N5, so the same AVRCP keys). Bluetooth earbuds send AVRCP play / pause / next / previous, the phone turns them into key events and gives them to the session that played most recently. TEST BUILD: `keyMsg` shows a toast for every earphone key and for what it did (1 / 2 / 3 presses); remove it when the user says the buttons work. Research: Android sends media keys only to the app that played most recently; Media3 1.3.1 does not turn a double press of HEADSETHOOK into next (fixed later, androidx/media #1493) and has no triple press = previous, which other apps do themselves. Not done: starting playback from a button press when the player service is not running (needs `onPlaybackResumption`). NOT compiled here (no javac) and not tried on a phone: let GitHub Actions compile; if the build fails, look at this block first.
- Tests updated (tests/homecfg.test.mjs), 159 pass. Not run on a phone.

- Settings > Profile: a name field right beside the profile picture. Home shows a different message for each hour of the day (`src/greeting.js`, 24 hours x 210 messages, tested in `tests/greeting.test.mjs`); without a name the message just drops it.

- Settings > Home screen (`src/homecfg.js`, tests/homecfg.test.mjs): show / hide / reorder 13 Home sections, cards per row (6/12/20), card size, greeting / counts / quick buttons switches, Reset. New sections: Jump back in (last played song) and Your numbers (plays, favorites, playlists, top artist; off by default). Saved in `set.home`.
- Home personalization (`src/homecfg.js`, tests/homecfg.test.mjs; UI in `homeset` in `src/main.js`, handlers `hs-*`): `set.home.pins` = pinned playlist / artist / album sections (key `pin:<id>`, up to 12, listed in `order` like any section, drawn by `pinSec` in the Home screen); `set.home.tod` + `todHidden{morning,afternoon,evening,night}` = which sections are hidden in each part of the day (`periodOf`: morning 5-11, afternoon 12-16, evening 17-20, night 21-4; Home rebuilds itself when the part of the day changes); `names` / `qnames` = your own titles for sections and quick buttons; `qb` / `qhidden` = quick button order and hidden ones (new `top` = Play most played, hidden by default). Old saved settings load fine (every new field has a default). Not run on a phone or in a browser here: only the unit tests and a syntax check.
- Home live refresh + Apple Music style rows: `refreshHome()` in `src/main.js` rebuilds `#main` in place (keeps scroll and greeting, waits 4 s after a touch) from `countPlay`, `favToggle` and `apicDone`. New sections `heavy` (Heavy Rotation), `favmix`, `newmix` (`snap.favmix`, `snap.newmix`, played through `listOf`). Update checker, keystore, signing and password code were NOT touched in this change (only greeting.js, homecfg.js, main.js Home code, app.css, tests, the two .md notes).

- Greeting (`src/greeting.js`): every hour has its OWN 210 messages (15 openers x 14 closers written for that hour only, 5,040 in total, none shared between hours; `{t}` = the hour), a new one each time Home is built, never the same twice in a row. Test checks 200+ unique per hour and that no message appears in two hours, with and without a name.
- Name: shown as plain text beside the profile picture; tapping it opens the editor (Save, Cancel, Remove name). No name yet = editor open.
- Not run on a phone.

## v59: Equalizer goes to Real Sound Technology (user: Sounds and vibrations > Real Sound Technology > inside it)
Real Sound Technology is two levels down (Sound & vibration > Real Sound Technology). `LiquidNative.openEqualizer` (1) `realSoundRoute()` looks for an EXPORTED Settings page whose class name or label says "Real Sound" and opens it in one step; a page called "Sound effects" is the weaker fallback; (2) else opens `android.settings.SOUND_SETTINGS` (Sound & vibration) and the toast says "Tap Real Sound Technology there." Every sound-related Settings page found is logged as `equalizer: candidate <package/class> label=... exported=...` (Settings > Copy log), so the real class name can be read there.
Limit: the Real Sound page may not be exported to other apps. If it is not, the user lands on Sound & vibration and taps one row. The next fix needs the `candidate` lines from the log.
Phone test: 1. Settings > Playback > Equalizer. 2. Real Sound Technology opens (or Sound & vibration + toast). 3. Change Smart / Movie / Game / Music. 4. If not in Real Sound: Copy log, find `equalizer:` lines.

## What changed (by ticket)
**A overlays**: A1 `closeNP()` in tm-album / tm-artist / tm-artist-go. A2-A4 `.toast` now top, z 95, wraps, max-width (end of `app.css`). A5 `#app.dragmode` hides tab bar + mini + fade while a playlist row is dragged; auto-scroll uses the screen edge. A6 `.lyr-now` is `visibility:hidden` until shown. A7 closed `.np` is `visibility:hidden` (after the slide). A8 `queueSheet()` swaps only `.qlist` rows when the sheet is open (keeps animation + scroll; `#qnow` is the "Now playing" line). A9 pick list + queue rows have no entrance animation. A10 `both` -> `backwards` on the row entrance animations.
**B**: B1 `scroller()` defined. B2 `snap.recent` / `snap.top` = the list the screen was built from (`listOf` uses it). B3 picker pages by 60 (`pickN`, "Show more" = `pick-more`). B4 search keeps the index, caps at 200 (`MAXR`). B5 `snip()` cuts decoded text, then escapes. B6 `NOALBUM` fallback in `trackAlbum()` / `mosaic` / album screen. B7 `lyFollow` only for synced lyrics. B8 `lrc.js` shifts word times per stamp. B9 `Native.moveItem` also moves the song inside `s.orig` while Shuffle is on.
**C**: C1 title regex no longer has `with`. C2 `with`, `vs`, ` x `, ` / ` moved from STRONG to WEAK (split only if a piece is a known artist; at least half the pieces must be known). C3 artist page lists albums they feature on; empty headings hidden. C4 `mainOf()` strips "feat." for album artist + fallback `pickMain()`; Java sends `albumArtist:""` when untagged and the new field `aaTag` (true = real tag). C5/C6 each song keeps its own `genres[]` (case-insensitive, `;` and ` / ` split); album genre = most common; `genreTracks` / new `genreAlbums`. C7 same-name albums in one folder split only on different real album-artist tags (`aaTag === true`; old cached scans never split until the cache is rebuilt: v61 bumped the cache file to `library-v7.json`, so every install re-reads its tags automatically on first launch; "Rescan library" alone never re-reads unchanged files). C8 Java `aaTag()` reads ALBUMARTIST / ALBUM ARTIST / ALBUM_ARTIST. C9 `letterOf`: Latin A-Z, own capital for Cyrillic / Greek etc., one section each for kana (あ), Han (漢), Hangul (한), else #.
**D**: D1 `glidebar.js` handles keyboard click (detail 0) + `aria-current`; nav has a label. D2 `keepFocus()` + identical-markup skip in `renderMini` / `renderNP`; double render on track change removed. D3 `syncModal()` (end of `main.js`): sheets / Now Playing / Lyrics are `role=dialog`, layers below are `inert`, focus moves in and back. D4 Escape = `closeTop()` (Android Back uses it too). D5 toast `role=status`. D6/D7 aria-labels with the song title on ✕ and ⋯. D8 lyric lines `role=button tabindex=0` + Enter / Space. D9 OS Reduce Motion read in `applySettings` (`data-motion`), CSS covers slide-in + Now Playing. D10 unsung word opacity .75. D11 `.sr:focus-visible` shows the arrows. D12 settings rows use `span.tx` inside buttons. D13 aria-labels on #nm / #pq / #q. D14 `aria-pressed` on Canvas / Sleep / A-B (in `a11y.js`).
**E**: E1 Theme row removed. E2 `window.__lf` only in the browser build. E3 Cancel first in Clear history. E4 "Sort Albums" capitalised. E5 rail code + unused CSS removed. E6 grab button is `.grabbtn`, bar span stays `.grab`.

## v56: playing line (seek bar) slow + glitchy
Cause: every player report (150 ms; this "BUG-282" is a different bug from the PlayCounter BUG-282 above) rewrote `width`/`left` (layout) + text, and the text write woke the `MutationObserver` at the end of `main.js`, which re-ran `applyA11y` over the whole app + `syncModal` ~12x/s. Also seeks fired on every pointermove and stale reports pulled the knob back.
Fix: `progress()` now only corrects a clock; one rAF (Now Playing open + playing) moves `#fill > i` and `#thumb` with `transform`; text / aria only when they change; observer ignores `#tl,#tr,#sleeptxt`; drag seeks once on release; `Native.seek` drops stale reports for <=0.8 s (`seekHold`); Java ticker 80 -> 150 ms (unbuilt, constant only). Check on device: smooth bar, drag + release, lyrics still on time, A-B loop.

## v56: Canvas black bars
Some Canvas clips have black bars baked INTO the picture, so `object-fit:cover` still shows them. `cvScan()` (main.js, Canvas section) samples real frames (96 px canvas, first ~6 s, union of the non-black rows / columns), `cvFit()` sizes + centres the media in px so the picture area covers the square (also works where a WebView ignores object-fit). Falls back to plain cover when the frame cannot be read (`_tainted`) or the guess is under 35 %. Check on device: a letterboxed clip fills the square; a clip without bars looks unchanged; rotating / resizing keeps it filled.

## v56 part 2: Equalizer + audit
**Equalizer (OPPO Reno 7 / ColorOS = Dolby Atmos with Smart / Movie / Music / Game):** the old code only sent the standard "audio effect control panel" action, which ColorOS does not answer, so nothing opened. `LiquidNative.openEqualizer` now (1) looks for installed sound-effect apps by package name (`dolby`, `soundeffect`, `audioeffect`, `effectcenter`, `soundalive`, `misound` ...) and starts their MAIN / settings-like exported activities (+ last guess `com.dolby.daxappui/.MainActivity`), (2) the standard panel with our audio session, (3) system Sound settings (resolves `via:"settings"`, the page toasts what to look for). Every try is written to the app log as `equalizer: ...`. `scripts/install-native.sh` adds `<queries>` + `QUERY_ALL_PACKAGES` to the manifest (Android 11+ hides other apps otherwise). NEVER tried on a phone: if it still fails, read the `equalizer:` lines in Settings > log.
**Audit fixes:** (1) `lrcFor` matched ANY .lrc >= 55 % alike, so "Track 2" got the lyrics of "Track 1" (wrong lyrics + wrong timing). Now exact / same name minus track number / one name contains the other with the same digits / >= 85 % alike with the same digits. Needs a Rescan to re-match. (2) Settings "Lyrics" row had `</div>` closing a `<span>` (broken markup). (3) New `onSeek` event (native.js `real.seek`): the bar + lyrics move at once on a seek while PAUSED (the player only reports while playing). (4) Probes: `java-probe-gain.py` now compiles the new equalizer code against stubs, `java-probe-scan.py` had a missing `lrcPlain` stub (failed already in v55).
**Static checks run (all clean):** every `data-act` has a handler, every `I.icon` exists, every `N.method` called from JS exists in Java, every queried `#id` exists.
**Read, no bug found:** lrc.js, sleep timer + fade (PlaybackService / SleepTimer), shuffle.js, queue ops in native.js, settings wiring (contrast / refract / motion / autoplay / autoCheck / lyric size / ReplayGain).
**NOT checked:** library scan details beyond lyrics matching, playlist export / import, sorting / A-Z rail, Song Info tiles, notification / MediaSession on a real phone, anything needing a device.

### v229 lyrics "Cover colour mix" is a real gradient
WHAT: src/main.js: new tintMeshGrad(mesh) builds a CSS gradient (vertical gradient from the mesh row averages + up to 5 soft radial glows at the cells that differ most from their row: a blush, lips, an earring). lyApplyTint sets --lyr-mix to it (was a small blurred picture). New test tests/lyrics-mix-gradient-v229.test.mjs. DO NOT REDO: no blur, no cover picture / GIF / video as the lyrics background (the owner plans that as a SEPARATE feature).

## v209 palette hair fix
- WHAT: the Cover colour palette now reads the hair itself. New `tintPaletteAddHair` + `tintPaletteHairFrames` in `src/main.js`, run LAST in `tintPaletteAuto` (after Consolidate). It takes warm brown/gold pixels (hue 35-100, chroma .03-.16, L .22-.78) of the top 26 % band and the two outer 20 % side bands, adds their TRUE median colour (lit + shaded tone when clearly different) at their real place, marked `hair: true`. `tintPaletteGrad`'s `rgb()` skips the chroma boost / 1.08 lift for `hair` colours.
- WHY: hair was mixed into the skin colour (middle of the face) or boosted / placed elsewhere.
- FILES: `src/main.js`, `tests/lyrics-palette-hair-v209.test.mjs`, docs.
- DO NOT REDO: do not move the hair step before Consolidate / Dedupe (they merge it into the skin); do not hard-code any picture's colour; version number untouched.

## v209b hair dot + small glow
- Hair place = densest real spot of the hair colour (not a centroid). Hair glow is a small accent (40% x 27%, on top) so it never covers the screen. A full palette never drops a small (<3 %) or vivid colour (blush, olive iris) for hair. DO NOT REDO: no big hair glow; keep the hair step last.

## v210 coverage + relocate
- WHAT: `tintPaletteAddCoverage` (a real colour AREA the palette misses, e.g. a shirt: far from the palette > .06, >= 0.8 %, solid, different hue/chroma or lightness; true colour at its densest spot; max 2; a full palette gives up its most redundant plain colour only when that costs less than the gain; `cover: true`, drawn as a small accent glow) and `tintPaletteRelocate` (a colour whose place holds none of it moves to the densest real spot of it; areas >= 12 % and hair stay), plus the shared helpers `tintPaletteReadPx` and `tintPaletteSpot`. Order in `tintPaletteAuto`: ... Consolidate -> Coverage -> Hair -> Relocate.
- WHY: the green-grey shirt was missing; blush / lips / background dots sat on the wrong spots.
- DO NOT REDO: no picture-specific colours or places; keep the solid-area and different-colour gates (they stop outlines and dark shades being added).

## v211 true blush colour
- A small rosy accent (blush, lips; share < 6 %) was boosted 2.4 x in chroma (tintPaletteAddAccents) and turned a dusty rose into a red. `tintPaletteRelocate` now ends by setting such a colour to the median of the pinkest 40 % of the real pixels (same hue, 9 x 9 px) around its place, with a x 1.3 boost only (`trueTone: true`). DO NOT REDO: do not drop this and keep the 2.4 boost for pink; olive eye / extras are unchanged.

## v212 low-quality (32 x 32) path
- `tintPaletteAuto` now runs Coverage / Hair / Relocate on the 32 px frames too when a picture has no 96 px frames (`fw = 32`; d.length multiple of 4096). All pixel distances scale with W / 96; at 32 px the solid test needs 3 neighbours (not 5) and the pixel floor is x .6. Tiny details the 32 px read has already lost (eye, blush) cannot be recovered there. DO NOT REDO: keep `fw` (not hiW) in those three calls.

## v247 (palette: colours the palette used to miss, for ANY picture / GIF / Canvas video)
- Owner's complaints (tested on stills, a GIF, a WebP, an MP4): a faint blush came out as a strong red or was missing, a silver headband, black hair vs lit skin, a chrome / silver sheen and a copper tone were missing, a tiny accent was shown as 0.0 %.
- `src/main.js`, after `tintPaletteAddHair`: NEW `tintPaletteTrueTone` (an over-vivid accent swatch goes back to the real colour of its hue family, never more vivid than before; two swatches that become the same colour are one; every swatch shows at least 0.4 %), then NEW `tintPaletteAddMissing` (the sensitive safety net: every pixel not covered by a palette colour is binned, neighbouring bins are joined so a smooth blush / metal sheen is ONE area, the best solid area >= 0.08 % (coloured) or >= 0.3 % (grey / silver / black) is added in its true colour at its densest spot, up to 4; a full palette drops its least useful colour only if the newcomer is clearly worth more). A silver / grey pixel is never "covered" by a pale blue, and a same-hue colour only covers a pixel of similar vividness. `tintPaletteRelocate` is still last.
- Tests: `tests/lyrics-palette-missing-v247.test.mjs` (new, 7 tests), wiring regex in `tests/lyrics-palette-coverage-v210.test.mjs` updated. The full suite has the same 22 failures it had before (native / updater tests that need a device), nothing new.
- Lab used for the PNGs: the real `tintImagePx` / `tintVideoPx` / `tintPaletteAuto` / `tintPaletteGrad` code run in headless Chromium.
